# P4 — The smoothing phase (2026-10-19)

> **Parent:** `docs/charter.md`, the root spec; this document is its child at level **P4** (charter §3 "P4" paragraph, opened 2026-10-19; §19's three lines of 2026-10-19; the P3 spec's §9 "Deferred (may return; the P4 seed)" and §13 "Carried to P4, not waived", `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-12-p3-co-work.md`). Parent pin: `docs/charter.md` at master 6db299a01 (§3 as amended 2026-10-19, §19 through 2026-10-19). **Consumes:** P3's landed contracts X23 to X32 (charter §13) and the P3 interface ledger's names (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-12-p3-interface-ledger.md`); the owner's observations after the sitting with the architect's routing (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p3-finish/owner-observations-2026-10-19.md`, obs 1 to 9); the recomposition record's P4 seed (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p3-finish/recomposition.txt` §7.7); the owner's 80 sitting candidates (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p3-finish/sitting-findings.md`) and the owner aid over them (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p3-finish/sitting-visual/candidates/status.html`: 23 fixed, 33 P4, 17 no row, 2 keep, 2 pass, 3 env); the four P4 notes of 2026-10-19, each cited below by its intended repository path and today at `/tmp/maws-review/`: the design notes (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4/design-notes.md`; `p4-design-notes.md`, every owner answer and architect ruling, dated), the edit-speed grounding with its sitting table (`research/p4-edit-speed-grounding.md`; `research/p4-edit-speed-grounding.md`, `research/p4-edit-speed/sitting-table.md`, the `p4-bench-*.py` scripts), the UX prior art (`research/p4-ux-prior-art.md`; `p4-ux-prior-art.md`) and the debt triage with its per-row JSON (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4/debt-triage.md` and `.json`; `p4-debt-triage.md`, `.json`); the tracker's rows at c395ff0d9 (286 rows; 64 P4-DESIGN, 92 WAVE, 87 OTHER-LANE, 20 HOLD, 16 CLOSE, 7 OWNER). Children dispatch per their grain hint and open by citing this document (path, child id, parent pin).

Status: **design presented to the owner 2026-10-19 ~20:3xZ after two question rounds and one answer round (Decision Log 1 to 6), approved in direction; track A narrowed by the owner's ruling of 20:36Z (Decision Log 8) and the Desktop comparison restored as a bounded parity bench by the owner's ruling of 20:41Z (Decision Log 10); revised the same day on the architect's rulings over the draft's open list (Decision Log 15) and on the adversarial review's sixteen findings (Decision Log 16); approved by the owner at 21:30Z on the approval ask's answers (Decision Log 17).** The owner's answers are Decision Log 1 to 4, 6, 8 and 10 **(owner)**; the architect's are 5, 7, 9 and 11 onward **(architect)** and can be overturned. Intake shape: coupled at the close and uncoupled in the building. One declaration over seven surfaces, one tool-time guard every paid live spec reads, one provisional layer two editors draw and one comment card two surfaces share need the joint view, so §4 is binding there; the design items themselves touch disjoint files and land independently, so the rest of §4 is advisory inheritance a child may overturn with evidence. P3 stays open beside this document until the owner's declaration after a week of daily use (P3 §13); the two phases share the tracker and the ledgers' allocation table and no other file.

## 1. Purpose

**Higher purpose.** MAWS is a general workstation for supervising Claude Code agents, built to the baseline of the reference apps and then past it (charter §1, D-5, D-20). P0 reached the baseline, P1 moved the agent workflow out of the terminal, P2 made the daily surfaces at least as good as Claude Desktop's, P3 added the co-work layer: documents, decks, designs, design systems and dashboards with a store, a protocol and a history. The owner's verdict at P3's sitting (P3 §13, M.1): "not yet … the structure is very solid … we just have to smooth things out". No task named still needs Claude Desktop or a terminal; what remains is that the surfaces are not yet fluid.

**What this phase is for.** P4 makes daily use fluid, fast and unsurprising on every surface the owner uses daily, the five artifact editors and the session-supervising surfaces alike (the sidebar, the transcript, tabs, the panel, notifications, the agents views), at the level of Google Docs, Google Slides, Canva, Claude Desktop and Codex (charter §3 P4; the owner, Decision Log 1). Where a reference does a thing well, MAWS does the same thing, established from the reference's own behaviour (`research/p4-ux-prior-art.md`: the vendor's documentation, open-source implementations and the readable sources, with live checks where nothing is published) and then proven on MAWS itself by live user tests: fresh agents operating a dedicated build through `cua_repl` in the owner's real scenarios, using every corner, trying to break it, comparing the same task in the reference app, and reporting to the owner, who gives the verdict (Decision Log 1, 3). The phase starts from P3's carried list and the owner's nine observations: comments beside the passage, click-to-edit on slides and designs, the main-area float, cross-surface tab drag, artifact search, the per-session launch profile, the inline artifact link, the direct send from the editor header, Drive through the gws CLI, Google Fonts fetched once.

**The measure (Decision Log 1, 3, 8, 10).** "Done to this extent": the design items landed; the agent's edit shown as it is written, with MAWS's own tool time under a guard the paid live specs enforce (the owner's worry was MAWS's tool performance and nothing else, Decision Log 8) and the parity question against Claude Desktop answered by one bounded bench whose result decides whether the deferred edit-protocol levers return (Decision Log 10); the debt waves landed beside the design; each of seven surfaces passed its loop (an assessment round, the owner's verdict, an enhancement wave, a re-assessment, the owner's pass); then the owner's declaration after daily use. UX improvement is a hill climb that is nearly endless (the owner, Decision Log 1), so the phase ends at a stated extent, read as how far it took the surfaces rather than as completeness, and carries what remains forward as tracker rows or jobs.

**What it must not be.** Not new breadth: sharing, simultaneous editing, a web-hosted MAWS and the teachers product are P5 (charter §3, the owner 2026-10-19; the P3 spec's §9 keeps them as P5's seed). Not a rewrite of P3's structure: the store, the editors, the protocol and the history stand (P3 §4.10, binding); P4 changes behaviour inside them. Not a regression on P3's §2: every item verified at wave S stays verified, and a P4 landing that trips a P3 e2e group fixes the regression, never the expectation, unless a dated line here says the behaviour changed. Not a write under the owner's `~/.claude` or `~/.claude.json` (D-1; the launch profile goes through the launch's own options). Not an effort decision or an inference optimisation (Decision Log 8: slowness from anything but MAWS's own tools is accepted; the parity bench of Decision Log 10 asks one bounded question, whether Desktop is equally slow on the same edits, and is not a close criterion by itself). Not a close on agents' opinions alone: an assessor's findings are evidence for the owner's verdict, never the verdict (Decision Log 1). Not an open-ended loop: a surface closes on the owner's pass, and the phase on the owner's declaration.

## 2. Acceptance

P4 closes when every item below is observed on the owner's Mac with the installed engine and a development-signed build (the agents' live runs under scratch config homes stand for what the owner does not run, P1 §15's rule; the loop's assessors stand for the sitting), and the owner then declares "done to this extent" after daily use. Items are observable behaviour; the children's gates refine them. The item's numbers are proposed from the research and are set by the child's plan after its live check where one is named.

1. **A document's comments sit beside the passage.** With the window wide, every open thread of the shown tab is a card in a margin right of the page, its top at the passage's first line, cards stacked without overlap in document order; clicking a passage or a card activates that thread, which sits exactly at its anchor, shifted slightly left, its neighbours pushed; typing above a passage moves its card the same frame; scrolling runs no layout; an agent's edit, selection or reply never changes the active thread. Narrower, cards shrink, then become an icon rail at the anchors (icons that collide merge into a count), then today's drawer under a 720 px body. View › Comments offers Expand, Minimize and Hide, persisted; an All-comments panel filters Open, Resolved, All, This tab, All tabs, a search; opening it takes the cards out of the margin and closing it brings them back with the same active thread. The page is centred when no thread is open and moves left when the first appears (the owner, Decision Log 6). ⌘⌥M comments on a selection; in a card R replies, E resolves, J and K move, U or Escape returns to the text; Escape on a non-empty draft keeps the draft (Decision Log 5). Resolving removes the card and keeps the mark; a passage deleted shows its thread as "The commented passage was removed", and undo brings the card back. (P4-B1; the prior art's L1 to L7, P1 to P6, R1 to R6, N1 to N6, Z1 to Z6, C1 to C8, K1 to K4.)
2. **A slide's or a board's comment is a pin and a popover.** One pin per commented element at its top right, following the element's moves in the same frame; a click opens a popover placed by `placeCard`, one at a time; Escape, a click on the pin or on the empty stage closes it and selects nothing; a non-empty draft survives closing; resolving the last thread removes the pin, deleting the element keeps its threads as orphans; the filmstrip badges a slide with open threads; presenting shows no pins. The card inside the popover is the document's card (X36). (P4-B2; the prior art's V1 to V15.)
3. **Slides and design boards edit on the first gesture.** A first click selects a text element and the editor underneath is already focused with the caret hidden; a second click places the caret; a drag of 3 px moves; a double-click anywhere edits the deepest text under the pointer in one gesture, a grouped heading and a table cell included, with the word selected; a printable key on a selected text element replaces its text; Enter selects all; Escape steps out one level; Backspace on a selection deletes the element; a Korean composition started on a selected, not yet editing, element lands composed, the first syllable intact, on the real Mac through the 2-Set input method (the spike's proof before the plan). A double-click on an inactive design board activates it and edits in the same gesture. (P4-B2; the prior art's P1 to P12, K1 to K6, I1 to I5, L1 to L8; the owner's choice of Canva's select-first model, Decision Log 6.)
4. **A named Google Fonts face is fetched once into the deck.** Naming a Google Fonts family in `deck.json` fetches it once into the deck's assets if its licence is OFL, after which the editor, the presenter and every export render it offline; a non-OFL face is refused by name and renders through the fallback as today. (P4-B2; the owner, Decision Log 4.)
5. **The chat yields to a wide editor.** In a main split with a session beside an artifact, dragging the divider toward the session holds the session pane at 320 px, and below 160 px the editor spans the main area live and reversibly within the drag; on release the session becomes the floating card at the bottom right of the main area (the panel's card sits at the same corner) with its draft and scroll intact and exactly one composer mounted; Dock returns the split to its ratio at pointer-down; the editor's header has an Expand button that does the same; a window resize never floats or docks; relaunch restores the floating split as the panel's float restores. A float with no session in front stays refused with its reason (wave S F6). (P4-B3; the prior art's §3.3 table and round trip.)
6. **An artifact moves between the panel and a main tab by drag.** Dragging an artifact main tab onto the panel moves it there (the main tab closes, the Artifacts tool shows the record); dragging the panel's record or its tab onto a pane or the right edge moves it to a main tab; a drop on a surface already showing the artifact activates that view and closes the source; a plain drag never adds a view and nothing is ever nowhere; Escape or a release off any target changes nothing; the live view (scroll, selection, unsent text, a comment draft) is carried, or the move is refused with a reason shown, the same reason that disables the menu item; the drag is labelled with its outcome. A pinned panel Artifacts tool returns after a relaunch with its record, and a relaunch never flashes "No open tabs". (P4-B3; the prior art's INV-1 to INV-6, T1 to T20, Z1 to Z7, C1 to C4, S1 to S6, M1 to M5; the owner, Decision Log 6: move only.)
7. **The agent finds and names artifacts.** Asked about something it never opened, the agent calls `artifact_search` and finds it by words in its title or text, Korean particles and short terms included, every word required, titles weighted, with a snippet and a `read` object that opens a document at the block or a deck at the slide; every session is told the `[title](maws-artifact:<id>)` form whether or not it loads an artifact tool, and a link with `#v<version>`, a tab or a block fragment opens there; `show_pane` can open a document at a tab or block. (P4-B4; the prior art's §5.3, §5.4.)
8. **Delivery without a turn.** The editor header's Send to delivers the shown artifact to Notion through MAWS's own connection with no Claude turn and shows a receipt; Drive delivery runs through the gws CLI after a setup the owner completes inside MAWS, MAWS holding no Google credential of its own; one owner-run delivery each. (P4-B5; the owner's R.2 H and R.3:1.)
9. **The agent's edit is seen as it is written.** From the first streamed delta of an `artifact_edit` or `artifact_publish`, the editor shows the agent's presence on the target and, as the text arrives, a provisional layer in the agent's colour at the target block, slide or board, never written to the document, replaced by the real text only once that write has integrated in the editor (never a gap, even while the owner composes) and withdrawn without trace when the call is refused; the human's composition is never touched; the time to the first visible change on the sitting's document tasks is first-token time (seconds) rather than the call's completion. A session launches with a per-session profile of plugins, output style and hooks chosen in MAWS and applied through the launch's own options, the user scope the default, nothing written under `~/.claude`. (P4-A2; the owner, Decision Log 6, amending D11's commit-time publish; Decision Log 8.)
10. **MAWS's tool time is guarded.** Every paid live spec that drives an `artifact_*` call reads that call's MAWS execution time from the engine-host journal (`tools/call` in to `control_response` out) and fails above the bound X34 names; the heaviest known cases (a 24-board design publish, a 100-slide deck publish, a 200-page document batch) are in the guard's own fixture. (P4-A2; X34.)
11. **The parity question is answered.** Four edit tasks (a one-word document fix, a paragraph rewrite, a one-slide date change, adding a slide) have run in MAWS in a fresh session and in Claude Desktop on equivalent artifacts, both pinned to the same model and effort (the owner's usual setting; where Desktop's arm cannot be pinned, MAWS's arm for that cell is pinned to Desktop's actual setting as a measurement-only condition and recorded as such), at least five repetitions per cell with medians and the spread reported, timed from both apps' engine records by one parser, with the per-edit breakdown (requests, input tokens, first-byte wait, generation time and output tokens, server hold, tool execution) and the verdict under the one threshold fixed beforehand: MAWS's median send-to-edit-landed above 1.2 times Desktop's on the same task means Desktop is faster, otherwise parity; parity closes the question and tracker row 296; Desktop faster means the breakdown names the cause and the matching deferred levers have landed as the edit-protocol child P4-A3, a cause outside those levers becoming a tracker row; a kind Desktop lacks reports its components without a verdict. (P4-A1; conditionally P4-A3.) The owner's ruling at approval (Decision Log 17): the verdict is a close criterion; Desktop faster on a judged task means P4 closes only when P4-A3 has brought MAWS's median send-to-edit-landed within 1.2 times Desktop's on that task, re-measured on A1's harness.
12. **The debt waves.** W1 to W3 landed with their rows closed; the 16 CLOSE rows removed and the 7 OWNER rows ruled; the English-throughout locale sweep landed (every age, date and time in English on every surface); the wave over the owner's responses to the 80 sitting candidates landed. (P4-W.)
13. **Seven surfaces passed.** Documents; decks; designs and design systems; dashboards; the library and Spaces; the session-supervising shell; the transcript and composer: each has an assessment round by fresh frontier assessors on a dedicated build through `cua_repl`, a picture page, the owner's verdict, an enhancement wave, a re-assessment by fresh assessors and the owner's pass, with the spend reported per round. (P4-L; X39.)
14. **The close.** Recomposition on items 1 to 13; what remains written as tracker rows or jobs, each with its constraint; the charter's §3 and §13 amended; then the owner's declaration. (P4-F.)

## 3. Grounding baseline (2026-10-19, master 6db299a01)

From the P3 recomposition record, the four P4 notes (the grounding agents' read-only passes over c395ff0d9, which 6db299a01 follows by one docs-only commit) and the architect's checks:

- **What the owner felt and what the numbers say.** At the sitting the owner's N.1 edit turns ran 21 to 77 s from send to the last output, the first output after 5 to 22 s (owner-results R.3). The grounding joined the note session's transcript (engine 2.1.303), its X8 journal and the relay's per-request log (185 of 185 requests): MAWS's execution of every `artifact_*` call was **0.02 to 0.19 s** (`tools/call` in to `control_response` out), never more than 0.27 s in a turn; the deck's "14 s `artifact_publish`" and "11 s `artifact_reply`" were the API response stream held open with no bytes after the model finished the call (15.1, 14.3 and 11.1 s in the journal, the same as the relay's `upstreamLongestGapSeconds`), a server-side end-of-response hold MAWS received the call after; across 175 tool-calling requests the hold's median was 0.43 s, and only the five deck calls exceeded 3 s (`research/p4-edit-speed-grounding.md` §0, §2.3). A document edit (n = 9) averaged 21.6 s: 11.0 s waiting for first bytes over 4.2 requests, 8.4 s generating (about 40 % thinking at xhigh), 1.5 s of holds, 0.2 s of tool execution; a deck or design publish (n = 3) 66.2 s, with whole slide or board files written as tool input (1,368 to 3,326 output tokens for a date, a font and one line) and 18 s of holds (§0, §3.1). The session ran at 527K to 555K tokens, about 82K of them duplicated guide payload (`artifact_describe_type` never marks a kind as guided, `src/engine-host/tool-server/artifact-tools.ts:111`, while create checks `deps.session.guided`) and seven copies of `DESIGN.md`; results are pretty-printed (`server.ts:52`) (§2.1). Model and effort came from the owner's `~/.claude/settings.json` (Opus 5.5, `effortLevel: xhigh`), not from MAWS, which passes no `--model` and no effort (§2.5).
- **Visible only at the call.** The host write commits at the tool call and presence is published at commit (D11 as read by wave S F19: held 4 s after the last batch, `PRESENCE_EDITING_MS`), while MAWS already receives every `input_json_delta` (9,465 frames in the journal, about 17 characters every 50 ms, `--include-partial-messages` and eager tool streaming on) and uses them only for the transcript's live row; the normaliser drops the tool name from `content_block_start` (`src/engine-host/normalizer.ts:265-269`). The 12:06 deck edit streamed its slide for 8 s and appeared 14 s after the model finished writing it (§2.4). Claude Desktop forwards streamed tool input in 16 ms batches but draws its same-engine artifacts only after the call too; what feels fast in Claude's products is claude.ai chat artifacts, one `old_str`/`new_str` call drawn while it streams, on a different engine (§1.2).
- **Claude Desktop has no faster edit primitive on the same engine.** Its server-hosted Slides, Design, Docs and Dashboard types publish whole files over the network (`POST /api/frame/deploy/direct`; 2.1.300 adds `edits:[{old_str,new_str}]` applied on the client and then published whole); its Cowork HTML artifacts are full rewrites behind an approval with a 3 s verify sleep; Cowork runs leaner than the sitting (a per-session `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` with no user-scope plugins, effort `medium`, eager streaming) (§1.1, §1.4). MAWS's document protocol is already a patch protocol (`replace_text` with a 178-character median input); its deck and design protocol is whole files, as Desktop's (§2.2).
- **The user scope at a launch.** `--setting-sources user,project,local`: 131 tools in `init`, 7 MCP servers, 47 skills, 13 plugins, the owner's output style, Stop hooks of 0.2 to 5.3 s per turn (31 s over 33 turns), PreToolUse hooks on Bash and ptc, the ptc detour and the cua self-driving the owner noted (candidates 6, 31, 74; tracker row 270, S-SESSION-SCOPE: the user scope stays each session's default and P4 adds a per-session profile through the launch options, the owner 2026-10-19). The engine honours `--settings` JSON for non-protected keys (`src/engine-host/launch.ts:149-189`) (§2.5, §5.1).
- **Comments today.** A document's threads are a right rail toggled by "Comments N" (`ThreadsRail.tsx`, P3-B D9: "the threads list is the editor's right rail tab beside History"), `beside` from 720 px and a drawer below (`rail-layout.ts`); the positioning a margin needs exists (`passageRect`, `CommentOverlayProps.locate` with a `layoutKey` on edit, scroll and resize) and only the composing card uses it; the deck and the design share one `CommentsRail` with pins at commented elements (`Pins.tsx`) that open the rail (owner-observations §1). The composing card does not follow the artifact's scroll (tracker rows 92, 93).
- **The click model today.** Selection is pure over the model: `pick()` at the current scope's level, `drill()` one level per double-click, `'edit'` only on a non-container text tag; printable keys are not handled except wave S F9's typed-key path, which excludes Hangul because a non-editable focused element has no input context on Chromium macOS (`Overlay.tsx:55-58,330`); the stage is one region with no element-level accessibility nodes; a grouped heading takes click, double-click, double-click, the obs-5 table cell four gestures (owner-observations §5; tracker row 293, high priority).
- **Layout today.** Floating is a right-panel state entered by the panel divider past its maximum (ending the drag 40 px past the bound), ⌥⌘B or the header's Float; the card draws the focused main tab's session, which is null for an artifact tab; the main split clamps at 25 to 75 % with no expand threshold (`SplitDivider.tsx:16-25`); the window minimum is 960 px. The main strip uses native HTML5 drag with `application/x-maws-tab`; the panel's tool strip has no drag code; a panel tab ref carries no artifact id (row 290); wave S made "Open in tab" a move and added "Show in panel" (F16). Several replicas of one artifact may be open (P3-L Decision Log 8). The main pane renders "No open tabs" before the layout store hydrates (row 291) (owner-observations §4, §7).
- **The agent's artifact context today.** 17 `maws` tools, 14 of them `artifact_*` (`src/shared/maws/tools.ts:29-37`); `artifact_list {query}` matches a title substring over the session lineage; main's FTS5 index (`library_text`) serves only the library UI and the mention picker, as one phrase ordered by recency with a 64-token snippet; the Space fields landed in wave S F5 (`artifact_create` takes `space`, `artifact_list` returns `spaceId` and `spaceName`; owner-observations §6 is stale on that); `maws-artifact:<uuid>` links render as chips (F14) with the instruction in the artifact tools' descriptions and the kinds' guides, no always-on instruction (row 303), the fragment parsed away (row 304); `show_pane` cannot open at a tab or block (row 139). The engine passes only plain MCP content to the model (no `search_result` blocks), caps a result at 25,000 tokens and a description at 4,096 characters (`research/p4-ux-prior-art.md` §5).
- **Delivery today.** Notion through MAWS's own connection (route D, P3-HD) is live from the library's Export menu through a session's Send to action; the editor header has no Send to; the Send to sheet's "You approve the connector in the session" is untrue under Auto mode (row 294); Drive was not run (row 295).
- **The tracker.** 286 rows at c395ff0d9: 92 WAVE (49 S, 43 M, no L; three waves by past throughput), 87 OTHER-LANE (desk 33, cua and the in-app browser 32, the gate lane 12, Electron and engine bumps 7, voice 2, the next settings version 1), 64 P4-DESIGN (21 riding a named design item, 43 design items in their own right: contract changes to X1, X2, X10, X13 and the adapter's provenance, features, product calls, the document adapter's carry design), 20 HOLD (upstream re-pins, a recorded case, a live probe, a sitting), 16 CLOSE with evidence, 7 OWNER (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4/debt-triage.md`). Wrong row text: eight paths or causes, four scopes, three counts; two duplicates (L149 = L241, L160 = L245); ten stale owners. Wave 1 is dispatched as four batches (W1-A renderer shell, transcript and cards, 15 rows; W1-B documents, 11; W1-C decks, designs, dashboards, the design-system page and DOCX, 13; W1-D the engine-host store, mirror, tool-server paths and exports, 14).
- **The 80 candidates** (`sitting-findings.md`; the owner aid): 23 fixed in wave S, 33 marked P4, 17 with no row, 2 keep, 2 pass, 3 environment; the owner's responses are pending (Decision Log 3).
- **References.** Codex desktop's readable build on this machine is `chatgpt-26.1013.31256`, which removed the gated composer-only overlay and keeps the floating chat as the only variant; `research/SOURCES.md` still names `26.928.40906`. Claude Desktop 2.42304.0 pins engine 2.1.295. Google documents none of Docs' margin geometry or Slides' click behaviour; Canva's help refuses scripted fetches; the numbers in §4 are Liveblocks', Codex's and VS Code's until the live checks replace them (`research/p4-ux-prior-art.md`, Limits).

## 4. Design

Everything below is advisory inheritance unless marked **[binding]** with its joint-view reason. Children expand their own sections in place; a child that overturns advisory content records a dated line in §12 under its id. The prior art's acceptance statements are cited by their ids (L1, P1, T1, V1, INV-1 and so on) and are the children's starting test lists, not binding text.

**4.1 The shape** **[binding — the close is one event over every child].** Four tracks and a loop. **A, the agent's edit speed:** A1 the parity bench against Claude Desktop, one bounded question whose verdict decides whether A3, the edit-protocol child, exists; A2 the agent experience: the edit shown as it is written, the per-session launch profile, the tool-time guard. **B, the design items:** B1 documents' margin comments; B2 slides and design boards (the click model, the pin popover, Google Fonts); B3 layout (the main-area float, cross-surface tab transfer, restore and hydration); B4 the agent's tool surface (`artifact_search`, the link instruction, fragments); B5 delivery (the direct send, Drive). **C, the debt waves** beside the design on the rows no design item rewrites: W1 (running), W2, W3 and WR, the wave over the owner's responses to the 80 candidates. **L, the live user tests**, after A to C and the 80 responses land: seven surfaces, each closing on its own pass. **The close** is §2 as written, not the sum of the children; the owner's declaration ends the phase "to this extent" and the remainder is carried forward as rows or jobs. The reference apps are the bar for behaviour (D-20): Codex for interaction and layout, Claude Desktop for the session and the agent's presence, Google Docs for comments and history, Canva and Google Slides for the canvas; MAWS invents only where no reference does the thing (the one-gesture reach into grouped text, the drag-to-float on a main split with a card) and says so.

**4.2 The agent's edit speed** **[binding on the parity bench's decision rule (stated before the run, so the result cannot be argued after it), on the provisional layer's four rules and on the guard's definition — two editors and every paid live spec depend on them; advisory on the profile's surface and the bench's mechanics].** A1 the parity bench; then three things in one child (A2); A3 conditional on A1.

*The parity bench (A1).* The owner's reasoning (Decision Log 10): if Claude Desktop is equally slow from server round trips and output time, MAWS has no problem; if not, something else is wrong, so it is worth running. The shape is the grounding's §4 cut to one question. **Tasks:** four, the prompt verbatim in both apps on equivalent seeded artifacts in each app's native form: B1 a one-word fix in a document's schedule paragraph; B3 a paragraph rewrite of about the same length; B5 a date change on one slide; B7 a slide added after slide 2 with a title and three bullets. **Arms:** MAWS in a fresh session (the owner's scope, the gateway route, auto mode) and Claude Desktop (a Code session, entrypoint `claude-desktop`, the same engine) on the kinds the owner's Desktop offers, both pinned to the same model and effort, the owner's usual setting (Opus 5.5 at the owner's `effortLevel`), no effort tuning; where Desktop's arm cannot be pinned to that setting (Cowork, the only home of the deck kinds when the Code session's `Artifact` tool is not gated on, runs a scratch config directory at effort `medium` under its own system prompt), MAWS's arm for that cell is pinned to Desktop's actual setting as a measurement-only condition and recorded as such, and no effort decision follows from it (Decision Log 8); **at least five repetitions per cell**, interleaved, fresh sessions, the cache state recorded, medians reported with the spread (the grounding's §4.6: server-side first-byte p90 runs to 3 to 6 s, so fewer runs cannot separate a 2 s difference). **First step:** confirm which artifact kinds the owner's Desktop account offers (the server-hosted Slides and Design types behind `cobalt_plinth`, Cowork's local HTML artifacts, eager streaming), read from the first session's `init` record, since the gates cannot be read from static code; the two deck tasks run on the nearest kind Desktop offers, stated in the result, and are never dropped, since the deck is where the sitting's 66 s was (Decision Log 15); a kind Desktop lacks altogether reports its components without a verdict. Desktop's artifacts live in one project in the owner's Anthropic account named for the bench and deleted after (X39; the architect names it to the owner). **Timing:** both apps' engine records through one parser (`research/p4-edit-speed/parse.py`'s reading of the transcript's `api-request` and tool records; Desktop's `audit.jsonl` and its session transcript), so the driver's latency drops out; Desktop is driven through `cua_repl` (its GUI is the only way to send), MAWS through the live-spec harness; the host wherever compute is free (the mini under the CI quiet flag, or the MacBook), the 1-minute load recorded. **The breakdown per edit:** requests, input tokens (the context size), first-byte wait, generation time and output tokens, server hold, tool execution; plus send to first token, send to the edit landed and send to turn end. **The decision rule, binding and fixed before the run, with no middle band:** on each task, MAWS's median send-to-edit-landed above 1.2 times Desktop's median means Desktop is faster; otherwise parity (the architect's proposed "about 20 %", Decision Log 10, made one threshold, Decision Log 16). Parity on every judged task closes the question and tracker row 296; Desktop faster on any means the breakdown names the cause, and when the cause is one of the deferred levers (patch publish for decks and designs, the request diet, the context diet; §9) that lever returns to P4 as the edit-protocol child A3, while a cause outside them (the relay's first-byte overhead, the user scope's tool count, a server hold) becomes a tracker row, never A3. Whether the verdict is a close criterion is the owner's at approval (the approval ask); until then its result is recorded and A3's existence is what it decides. The harness stays in the repo as the live-spec arm the guard reuses.

*The edit shown as it is written (X35).* The deltas are already on hand (§3). The host keeps the tool name on `content_block_start` (an additive X1 amendment through the ledger), reads the `artifact` key from the partial JSON of an `artifact_edit`, `artifact_publish` or `artifact_dataset` call as soon as it parses (it was the first key in every observed call) and publishes presence on that artifact from the first delta (this alone closes row 298); as the call's content arrives (an op's `new` or `markdown`, a file's text, a dataset) a tolerant streaming parse feeds a **provisional layer** the editors draw: in the agent's colour at the target (the block an op names or quotes; the slide or board file's element tree as far as it parses; the dashboard view), labelled as the agent's work in progress, batched at about 16 ms as Desktop batches its deltas. The hold stays editor-wide as P3 built it (row 138's block-scoped hold is not A2's; §9); rule 2 works with it. The four rules: **(1) never a Y write** — the layer lives beside the document and changes no version, provenance or history; it does change layout (provisional text grows its block), so its growth is a `layoutKey` change coalesced to one per animation frame, which B1's margin re-layout includes (Decision Log 16); **(2) withdrawn only when the real write has integrated** — the host's `committed` event carries the commit's seq (the write's origin), the renderer keeps the layer until its replica has applied that update, and under the composition hold the withdrawal is buffered with the Y frames the hold holds (`holdRemote`, X24; `src/artifacts/document/collab.ts`), so the agent's words never vanish during a human's composition and nothing flashes between two messages; **(3) withdrawn without trace on refusal** — a batch the host refuses (a stale `baseRevision`, a changed slide) takes its layer with it, and the transcript's refusal is the only record; **(4) the human's composition is never touched** — the layer never renders inside a block the human is composing in (the composition hold's block) and never steals focus or selection, which on the slide editor means the layer is drawn beneath the stage's ARMED and EDIT states (B2) and lands there only after B2's state machine exists (§7). This amends the sitting item D11 as wave S read it ("nothing is revealed for a batch that is then refused", recomposition §7.7's S-DOC-AGENT-PRESENCE-LIVE line): the owner chose to see the edit as it is written, provisionally, and accepts that a refused batch is seen and then withdrawn (Decision Log 6). Turn time is unchanged; what changes is the time to the first visible change, from 7 to 56 s at the sitting to first-token time.

*The per-session launch profile.* A MAWS-owned profile per session: the plugins enabled (the user's set or a subset), the output style (the user's or MAWS's own), the hooks (the user's or none), applied through the launch's own settings surface and nothing else, pinned (Decision Log 16): `--setting-sources` always keeps `user` (dropping it would shed the model, the effort, the permission settings and the gateway env with the plugins, against Decision Log 8); `--settings` carries flag-level `enabledPlugins` (per plugin true or false; the engine's precedence is user < project < local < flag < managed, `src/engine-host/session-start-hooks.ts:69`) and `outputStyle`; hooks only through `disableAllHooks` (the engine has no per-source hook switch) or as the code shows at A2; never by editing the owner's files (D-1; row 270's constraint); the user scope is the default, so nothing changes until the owner chooses (the owner, 2026-10-19, tracker c395ff0d9). No effort field: the owner ruled effort out (Decision Log 8). A2's acceptance includes a wire probe (R-7): a session launched with a profile shows in its `init` record the same model and effort as the plain user scope plus exactly the chosen plugins and style. Where the profile is chosen (the new-session composer, Session info, a per-project default) is the child's; it is one of the shell surface's items the loop assesses. An X6 amendment through the ledger; W2-A's L94 lands first (the launch options enter `inflight.ts`'s join rule). A2 is the child; the profile's chooser is one of the shell surface's items. **Amended 2026-10-21 (the owner's ruling, Decision Log 55):** one profile for every session MAWS launches, chosen in Settings › Engine and never per session (settings version 17's `engine.launchProfile`), read at each launch with no session record; under hooks none MAWS's start-time alias rides the appended prompt, since `disableAllHooks` silences the flag JSON's own hooks (Decision Log 50 corrected).

*The tool-time guard (X34).* The finding of §3 stays true by construction: every paid live spec that drives a guarded call (`e2e/*-live.spec.ts` under `MAWS_LIVE=1`) reads from the launch's X8 journal (`<userData>/engine-host/journal/<appSessionId>/<launchId>.jsonl`, each frame with its `at`) the span from the `mcp_message` carrying `tools/call` in to the `control_response` out, per call, and fails when any call exceeds the bound or the spec's median exceeds its half; the guarded tools are named (Decision Log 16): the store tools `artifact_create`, `artifact_read`, `artifact_edit`, `artifact_publish`, `artifact_dataset`, `artifact_comments`, `artifact_reply`, `artifact_resolve`, `artifact_list`, `artifact_search`, `artifact_describe_type`, `artifact_upload_asset` and `artifact_list_assets`; `artifact_export` and `artifact_screenshot` render through main's hidden window (P3 §4.8) and take a bound of their own, provisional from the live specs' recorded timings at A2's first run, since the store bound would guard nothing there; the guard is one shared helper in `test/live/support/`; the heaviest known cases are in a fixture of its own (E1's 24-board bench board, row 187's 61 ms host-loop hold; a 100-slide deck publish; a 200-page document batch from S7's corpus) that drives the tools over the wire through the scripted transport (`scriptedSession` and `scriptedTransport`, `src/engine-host/conformance/`), so the journal carries the `tools/call` and `control_response` frames the helper reads; and the bound is a named constant with its grounding beside it: 1,000 ms per call and the spec's median at 500 ms, provisional, both confirmed from the heavy fixture's first run at A2 (Decision Log 15). Row 296 closes on A1's parity result (Decision Log 10), and the guard is its standing successor as the regression check (Decision Log 8); the duplicated guide text (`artifact-tools.ts:111`) is a defect for a debt wave, not a design item.

**4.3 Comments: the document margin and the canvas pin** **[binding on the shared card (X36), on "only the human activates a thread" and on the view states' names; advisory on every number].** The owner's words (obs 1): comments beside the highlighted passage as in Google Docs, the open-and-close rail is not good UX. **Documents** take Docs' product surface built on the margin algorithm five open-source editors share, Liveblocks' `AnchoredThreads` the reference implementation (Apache-2.0, Tiptap and ProseMirror, MAWS's stack): a `margin` rail mode, `MarginThreads` rendered inside `.doc-page-frame` as an absolutely positioned column right of the page; a card per open thread of the shown tab at `top = passageRect(threadId).y` (the first line box of the earliest live range; a block thread at the block's top), ordered by document position, stacked with a strict gap token (8 px until Docs is measured), the active thread pinned at its anchor and shifted left by a token (12 px until measured), its neighbours pushed both ways (down: `max(anchor, previous bottom + gap)`; up: `min(anchor, next top − gap − height)`), clamped at the column top and growing the scroll height at the bottom so no card is lost; re-laid on `layoutKey` (doc, active thread, thread set, card resize, frame resize, tab, font load, and the provisional layer's growth coalesced to one per animation frame, X35) and never on scroll; moves caused by activation, add or resolve animate 200 ms, moves caused by edits never, and nothing under reduced motion; **only the human activates a thread** (a click on the passage or the card, the local caret, J and K, next and previous), never a remote selection or edit, because agents write constantly. Three width tiers reading the side space beside the page, never the window: cards shrink to a minimum, then an **icon rail** at the anchors (Docs' Minimize form; colliding icons merge into a count; hover previews; click opens an overlaid card), then today's drawer under a 720 px body. View › Comments: **Expand, Minimize, Hide**, persisted per user. The existing list becomes the **All comments** panel (Open, Resolved, All; This tab, All tabs; a text and author search; an optional "For you"), mutually exclusive with the margin cards as in Docs. The **margin is reserved only while a thread or a compose card is open on the shown tab**: the page is centred otherwise and moves left when the first appears (the owner, Decision Log 6; Docs' behaviour without its stale-left bug). Compose: an Add comment button beside a non-empty selection; a compose card active in the stack; ⌘Enter posts in place; blur when empty discards; blur with text keeps the draft inactive in the stack; **Escape on a non-empty draft keeps the draft** and returns focus to the text, Cancel discards (Decision Log 5; MAWS's `placement.ts` already keeps a hidden card's draft; a deliberate departure from Docs); one draft per document; closing with a draft asks. Keys are Docs' (⌘⌥M; in a card R, E, J, K, U; ⌃⌘ N then C and P then C). Resolve keeps the mark and lists the thread under Resolved; a deleted passage reads "The commented passage was removed"; undo restores. The composing card's `placeCard` gains a margin mode, which retires rows 92 and 93 by construction. The 200-page typing path (row 235, p95 16.8 to 17.2 ms against 16) is the risk: the margin re-lays only on `layoutKey`, and B1's plan measures the keystroke bound with the margin open. P3-B D9 is amended by this section (a dated line in P3-B's Decision Log at B1's landing). **Decks and design boards** take the pin-and-popover model of Figma and Canva (Slides shows a deck's comments as cards beside the slide and documents no pin; the plan's precedent was wrong there): a pin per commented element at its top right following the element in the same frame, a click opening a `ThreadPopover` placed by the pure `placeCard(host, pinRect)` moved under `src/artifacts/comments/placement.ts` (today `src/renderer/src/side/card/placement.ts`; the lint rule forbids `src/artifacts/` from importing the renderer, P3 §4.10; Decision Log 16) with the newest thread's reply field focused, one popover at a time, Penpot's close rules (Escape in layers: the draft, then the thread, then the cluster; a click on empty stage closes and selects nothing), Slides' keys, drafts kept across closing, orphans listed without pins, a filmstrip badge, no pins while presenting, pins closer than 32 screen px on a zoomed-out board merging into a count. **The shared card (X36):** one `ThreadCard` component in `src/artifacts/` draws the thread in the margin, the icon-rail overlay, the popover and the panel: the compacted inactive form (the first message and the two latest replies, no quoted passage), the active form with the reply field, the keys, the draft rule; placement is per surface.

**4.4 Slides and design boards: the click model** **[binding on the state machine's states and on the armed editor as the IME arrangement, proven by the spike before the plan; advisory on thresholds and selection details].** The owner's words (obs 5): editing takes two, three, double, triple clicks; adopt Google Slides' and Canva's proven mechanism as it is. The references disagree on the first click (Slides places a caret; Canva, Keynote, tldraw and Excalidraw select); the owner chose **Canva's select-first model** (Decision Log 6) because one rule serves the deck and the board, groups and tables stay movable as units, and agent-made decks are moved and resized often. The states: `IDLE`; `SEL(ids, scope)`; **`ARMED(id)`**, a selection of exactly one text target whose editor is already mounted, focused and editable with the caret hidden (`caret-color: transparent`) and a solid frame; `EDIT(id)` with a visible caret and a dashed frame; `CELLS(table, a, b)`; `PRESS` from pointer-down to a 3 px move or a release; `DRAG(move | marquee | resize | cells)`; a `composing` flag over `ARMED` and `EDIT`. The transitions are the prior art's T1 to T22: a first click selects the outermost element at the current scope (T1) and arms it when it is a single text target; a second click without a drag on the armed element places the caret at the point (T3); a 3 px move drags (T2; Chromium's Mac threshold, MAWS's today); **a double-click from any state edits the deepest text element or cell under the pointer, ignoring scope, with the word under the pointer selected** (T5; a departure from Canva's level-entry the owner chose: the option put to the owner at 20:3xZ read "더블클릭하면 그룹이나 표 안에 있어도 가장 안쪽 텍스트로 바로 들어가요", Decision Log 6); a printable key on `ARMED` clears the content and inserts the key (T10; PowerPoint's and Keynote's rule for cells); a keyCode-229 keydown clears the content and lets the IME compose in place, flipping to `EDIT` with no remount, no focus change and no DOM write near the composition (T11); Enter selects all (T12); Backspace or Delete on a selection removes the element (T14); Escape steps out one level, `EDIT` to `ARMED` to the parent to `IDLE` (T17, T18); a remote replica deleting the element drops the selection, held until `compositionend` while composing (T20); on a design board a double-click on an inactive board activates it and applies T5 in one gesture (T22). While `ARMED`, element shortcuts take priority over the editor, gated on `!isComposing && keyCode !== 229`; `beforecopy` and `beforecut` are cancelled so the Edit menu stays enabled; `historyUndo` and `historyRedo` are claimed before ProseMirror; F9's Hangul exclusion drops once `ARMED` exists. **Why the armed editor is binding:** Chromium on macOS gives a non-editable focused element no input context and tells AppKit about a newly focused editable only after a 100 ms timer, and any focus move during a composition ends it, so the only arrangement that keeps a first Hangul syllable intact is an editor focused before the first key (Handsontable's `imeFastEdit` lesson; AG Grid's and MUI X's open bugs; the owner's stray `ㅇ` in obs 4). **The spike (B2's first gate)** proves it on the real Mac with the 2-Set input method (the prior art's L1 to L8, typed by the owner's hands or a HID-level poster, since cua's posted keys bypass the input method: Decision Log 39) and records one trace as a fixture for the simulated cases (I1 to I5, a CDP session interleaving `imeSetComposition` and `insertText`); if the platform refuses (the content cannot be cleared on the first keyCode-229 keydown before the composition starts), replace degrades to append and the spike says so; if the owner, shown both side by side, prefers Slides' immediacy, caret-on-first-click applies to top-level text boxes only with a 6 px border band for moving. Tables: a click selects the table, a second click on a cell gives a caret, a double-click on a cell from any state edits, a drag across cells of the selected table selects a range (Keynote and Canva's model). Accessibility: element nodes in the tree (text named by its first words, images by alt, groups "Group, N items", tables native), positioned mirror nodes as Figma's, polite selection announcements outside `EDIT` (tldraw's format), focus following selection, Escape never landing on `body` (the prior art's A1 to A5); these are B2's acceptance if its plan can bound them, else a known item of the decks surface (§9). The inspector refreshes at every gesture's end (row 280) and keyboard exit from the stage (row 253) are part of the same handler rewrite; W1-C's L281 (the inspector's names) lands before B2 touches `Inspector.tsx`. The composition-hold e2e step (row 256) is reworked with the IME clause. The host-faithful drag path's 16 ms remainder (row 231: defer an arriving agent publish's application during a drag; acceptance ≤ 24 ms meanwhile) is the deck editor's gesture-path design and rides B2 with the drag case's republish seam (row 212). **Google Fonts (row 176):** a family named in `deck.json`'s `faces` as a Google Fonts link is fetched once by the host into the deck's assets when the Google Fonts metadata says OFL, after which the editor, the presenter and every export (HTML, PDF, PPTX's named variant) render from the asset offline; a non-OFL face is refused by name with the fallback as today (the owner, Decision Log 4); the licence is read from the family's directory in the google/fonts repository (`ofl/<family>/` with its `OFL.txt` fetched beside the faces; `apache/` and `ufl/` families refused), not from the Developer API (a key) or the CSS endpoint (no licence); the fetch path is the host's outbound fetch of the family's CSS (`fonts.googleapis.com/css2`) and face files (`fonts.gstatic.com`), written as X23 assets, so the engine host makes an outbound fetch on the owner's edit of `faces` (P3-HD's route D already opened that door for the host's own OAuth; the egress declaration is R-8); the ledger row sits under X27 (the deck model's `faces` entry gains a fetched-asset reference), the asset itself an X23 `artifact_assets` row (Decision Log 15). The deck's comments take the pin popover of §4.3.

**4.5 Layout: the main-area float, cross-surface tab transfer, restore** **[binding on X37 (the split's float state and the card's session) and X38 (move only, activate-existing, the refusal shared with the menu); advisory on the pixel numbers and the gesture's feel].** *The main-area float* (obs 4: as in Docs, when the editor widens past a point the chat should shrink into a floating widget at the bottom right). No product floats a chat on a width breakpoint; Codex desktop, MAWS's UX reference, floats it on a drag, and VS Code, Codex and Claude Desktop all collapse a view live when a drag would leave it under half its minimum, reversibly, restoring it at its pointer-down size. So: **pixel minimums replace the 25 to 75 % clamp** (`S_min = A_min = 320`, Codex's content minimum and Claude's drawer minimum); dragging the divider toward the session pane holds it at `S_min` in the dead zone between 160 and 320; below `T = S_min / 2` the artifact pane spans the region live, the session column hidden, nothing persisted; returning past `T` in the same drag disarms; **on release the session becomes the floating card at the bottom right of the main area** (the owner's "오른쪽 아래", Codex's default); **both floating cards sit at the bottom right** (Decision Log 15): B3 reads the panel card's current corner (bottom left by P3-G §D1.4) and aligns it if it differs, with a dated line in P3-G's Decision Log and in P3's §12 (P3 §4.9's card ruling is binding there) and an X30 amendment through the ledger; `floating` set on the split with `splitRatio` still `r0`; Dock returns the ratio at pointer-down; the artifact pane's header gains an **Expand** button with the same effect (Codex, Cursor, Claude Desktop and VS Code's Agents Window all have one) and a keymap action with no default chord; a pointer-cancel docks at `r0`; arrows, Home, End and a double-click on the divider never float; a window resize never floats or docks (VS Code #332314's rule); the artifact pane never collapses (it clamps at `A_min`). **The card shows the collapsed pane's session** (`cardSession` is the pane's, not the focused tab's; the root of candidate 58, fixed in wave S for the no-session case) with the transcript and composer, its draft and scroll carried (Claude's scroll rule: pinned stays pinned, else the same `scrollTop` when the content is unchanged, else the bottom), exactly one composer mounted; the card's Close leaves the pill with the unread dot as P3-G's does; a permission request or question raises the card (Codex forces its chat open on a pending approval); the card belongs to its split and hides on another tab set; A's last tab closing docks the session full width; S's tab closing removes the card; relaunch restores the floating split with the card open (panel parity); ⌥⌘B while a split card is up docks the split card first. Card geometry reuses P3-G §D1.4 (420 × min(60 % of the main body, 640), minimum 360 × 240, 16 px insets, never over the tab strip). The card's rect is reserved for the editors as the browser pane's column is (a bottom-right card over a deck covers the inspector; Codex keeps 118 px of bottom inset), the editor's response advisory. The panel divider's 40 px rule stays and is logged as debt rather than converged. The new card layer carries `data-markup-occluder` (W1-A's L257 note). *Cross-surface tab transfer* (obs 6 and 7: a sitting note open in the panel could not join the tabs; drag to move is blocked; so a duplicate). VS Code's rules, with the owner's choice: **a plain drag moves and there is no copy by drag** (Decision Log 6; "one artifact, one place" was the complaint; P3-L Decision Log 8's several replicas stay reachable through "Open in tab" from the list, which is not a drag); the panel's strip and body accept `application/x-maws-tab` when the dragged tab is an artifact and open it in the panel while closing the main tab; panel tabs drag with `application/x-maws-panel-tab {toolId, tabId}` and the main strip, a pane's body and `DropEdge` accept it when the instance shows a record; `dragging` gains `source: 'main' | 'panel'`; **a drop on a surface already showing the artifact activates that view and closes the source**, an unsent draft in the source (a comment draft, an unsent composer text) carried to the activated view under X36's one-draft rule, never lost (Decision Log 16); a session tab is never accepted by the panel and ignores every modifier between panes; Escape, a release off any target, leaving the window and a cancelled guard dialog change nothing; the drop highlight shows the resulting pane (the right half for `DropEdge`, the whole pane for append, one whole-panel highlight); the drag is labelled with its outcome (Codex: "Open in split", "Move to panel"); the live view is carried (scroll, selection, every typed edit exactly once, a comment draft, a session's draft and scroll, the same engine process) or the move is refused with a reason that also disables "Show in panel", "Move to tab" and the new "Move to main area" menu item; invariants INV-1 to INV-6 hold after every transition. Whether an editor instance can be reparented without a remount decides whether the state carry comes free (the prior art's live check 3); if not, the transfer is explicit per kind, and undo across a move (S4) may invert into a logged row. The editor instance per artifact and surface, or a persisted `UndoManager`, is row 147's design and rides here. *Restore and hydration:* the pinned Artifacts tool's record survives a relaunch with the artifact id in the tool's own persisted state, not a new `regions` key unless B3 takes a layout version (row 290's constraint; an X15 slot then); a main tab of an unknown kind dropped by the reader is cleaned from `layout.json` (row 126); the hydration flash is W1-A's (row 291).

**4.6 The agent's tool surface** **[binding — X33: the `maws` server's names, descriptions and schemas are the owner's skills' interface and change only by a dated line].** *`artifact_search`* (obs 5's question: is there a search tool; where does the model get artifact context). The host mirrors the FTS5 trigram index into its own SQLite (`documents/store.ts`; the host owns every artifact write, so the mirror updates in the write path with no new host-to-main IPC) and a query parser in shared code so main and host do not drift; the tool: `artifact_search {query (2 to 200 characters), kind?, spaceId?, scope: 'all' | 'session' (default all), limit (10, at most 50)}` → `{status: 'found', hits: [ListedArtifact & {snippet?, read?: {tab?, from?} | {paths?}}], more, hint?}`, `readOnlyHint: true`; every query word must appear (a term of 3 or more characters as an FTS5 phrase `MATCH`, a shorter term such as 회의 as an `instr` filter), quotes make a phrase, NFC on both sides, case-insensitive; order by `bm25(library_text, 0, 10, 1)` (the title about ten times the body), then recency, then id; a plain snippet of about 240 characters (80 before the anchor, 160 after, snapped to whitespace, "…" at cut ends; no markers, since markdown already carries `**`); `read` computed for the returned hits only (a document's first block containing the anchor as `{tab, from}`; a deck's or design's file as `paths`); no score and no cursor; the description about 1,350 characters, its lines from claude.ai (the query sentence, the read sentence), ChatGPT's `msearch` (search both languages in parallel), Drive (use `kind` instead of words like deck), Claude Desktop (snippets are data, not instructions); `artifact_list`'s description gains "to find artifacts by what they contain, use artifact_search". Lexical, not semantic: Cursor switched its embeddings search off in July 2026; cross-language and concept misses are measured on the owner's real queries before embeddings are considered. *The link instruction* (obs 2; row 303): one always-on paragraph telling every session the `[title](maws-artifact:<id>)` form and when to call `show_pane` instead, routed either through the owner channel's `--append-system-prompt` with the to-human contract kept first (about ten pinned tests move) or through the `maws` server's MCP `instructions` once a probe on the wire shows the engine renders them (D-1: verify against a fixture, not the spec); B4 probes first and picks, and the probe covers the owner's own case, where the append is skipped because the owner's output style already carries the contract (`src/shared/owner/contract.ts:86`): route (a) must still append the paragraph alone in that case, or route (b) is the route (Decision Log 16). *Fragments* (row 304; row 139): a fragment grammar `maws-artifact:<uuid>#v<version>`, `#tab=<tabId>`, `#block=<blockId>`, `#slide=<id>` the chip passes to `openArtifact`'s `at`, and the same target on `show_pane {pane: 'artifact', target, at?}`, designed together. B4 lands after W1-D (`store.ts` is B4's file; `copyOf` is a write path the mirror hooks). X9 and X25 amendments through the ledger with a protocol-probe fixture of the new `tools/list` entry; whether the SDK-hosted `maws` server's tools are deferred under `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` and whether `_meta["anthropic/alwaysLoad"]` applies to it is probed, not assumed.

**4.7 Delivery** **[advisory, except the owner's two rules: no Claude turn for a send, no Google credential of MAWS's own].** *The direct send* (the owner's R.2 H: "do we really have to open a session to export to Notion?"; row 294): the editor header's Send to (an X32 `actions[]` entry) opens a sheet naming the connection ("Connected through MAWS", route D's store, P3-HD) and the destination, exports the shown artifact in the connector's format through the export service (X31) and uploads through MAWS's own OAuth, with a receipt in the header and the library's row; no session, no model turn, no permission mode; until it lands the Send to sheet's interim copy states the session's permission mode (row 294's clause; S, may ride any renderer batch). *Drive through the gws CLI* (the owner's R.3:1; row 295): delivery runs the CLI the owner installs, with a setup the owner completes inside MAWS (the CLI's own login, its state kept by the CLI; MAWS stores nothing beyond what the CLI stores) and one owner-run delivery closes the row; a fixture regression covers the CLI's absence and a refused login.

**4.8 The debt waves** **[advisory; the triage's bins are the record, `docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4/debt-triage.md`].** Rows disjoint from the design items' files, in waves of four opus workers in isolated worktrees, one commit per row, class 1a through `scripts/ci-gate.sh <worktree> --gates static,unit,probes` and the named e2e groups on the mini, a task-reviewer round and its findings fixed, landed `--no-ff` with the records. **W1** (dispatched, four batches): W1-A renderer shell, transcript and cards (15 rows, among them rows 291, 292, 276, 197, 257's occluder note, 301, 134, 289); W1-B documents (11: the dialect's `-`/`+` and comment-mark escapes, the leading blank, "See N new changes" on every return, history words, carry labels, the card label's title half, tab keyboard paths, the paste asset, the HTML export ground, the undock race); W1-C decks, designs, dashboards, the design-system page and DOCX (13: the inspector's names before B2, the accent fill, filmstrip announcements, Fit, the uploaded-family alias, compiler bailouts, presenter focus, design history stops, the order settle, repeated bars side by side, the canon preview, the export author, DOCX orphans); W1-D the engine-host store, mirror, tool-server paths and exports (14, 13 changes: the import retry, the suggestion-attribution overlay, the staged listing, the duplicate's origin, four mirror rows, the named-path reads, `versionId` on `artifacts.assets` as a ledger X23 row, the export budget, header export names, the refresh log; **before B4**). **W2:** W2-A sessions and the engine-host disk path (7; L94 before P4-A2's profile); W2-B main services (5); W2-C the renderer remainder and browser-shared (7); W2-D harness, build, live and compiled coverage (12; L213 before B1 and B2 implement) and **the English-throughout locale sweep** (row 133: every age, date and time in English on every surface, `relativeAge` and the six en-US pins through `app/date-format.ts`, about 24 suites' expectations; the owner, Decision Log 4). **W3:** W3-A editors and exporters remainder (4); W3-B the measurement harness (4; L242 recalibrated after B2). **WR,** the wave over the owner's responses to the 80 candidates, cut when the responses arrive (33 are marked P4 today; the aid's "open only" filter is the owner's worksheet), with the same rules; if the responses have not arrived when A to C have landed, the architect asks the owner before L starts rather than starting L without them or guessing the responses (Decision Log 16). The CLOSE rows (16) leave the table at the triage's commit with their evidence; the OWNER rows are ruled (§12): L32 final, L133 the sweep, L156 closed, L176 to B2, L177 deferred, L181 the decks surface's owner items, L204 the gate lane's. The duplicates fold (L149 into L241; L160 into L245) and the wrong row text is corrected at the same commit. OTHER-LANE (87) and HOLD (20) stay out of P4's scope by their bins.

**4.9 The live user tests** **[binding — X39: one protocol every surface runs and every child's landing is measured by].** The owner (Decision Log 1, 3): the sitting's cua-driven agent gave many good UX suggestions; do the same, as a live user test in which the models do not just converse but use every corner of the product in real scenarios; no cost cap; the first round not now but after the discussed items and the 80 responses land, so round 1 runs at the frontier with every known defect addressed. **Seven surfaces:** documents; decks; designs and design systems; dashboards; the library and Spaces (delivery with it); the session-supervising shell (the sidebar, tabs, the panel and its tools, notifications, the agents views, the launch profile); the transcript and composer. The felt edit speed is not a surface (Decision Log 8); it is assessed inside each editor's surface as "the agent's edit is seen as it is written". **A round:** the host named at dispatch (the mini in a GUI hold, or the MacBook with the owner's go; §7); a dedicated development-signed build with its own MAWS user data directory, seeded with the surface's fixtures and the owner's real artifacts where the owner agrees, whose sessions read the owner's user scope like any MAWS session (`--setting-sources user,project,local` on the owner's configuration; the isolation is MAWS's, not the engine's), so the launch profile is assessable; two or three fresh frontier assessors (`astra` or `fable`), each operating the build through `cua_repl` on the owner's real scenarios for that surface (from the sitting script, the owner's notes and the owner's daily tasks named at the round's brief), instructed to use every corner, try to break it, compare the same task in the reference app (Docs, Slides, Canva, Figma, Claude Desktop, Codex) and skip the **known items** (the surface's open tracker rows and the design items not yet landed, listed in the brief so they are not re-reported); findings go to a **picture page** (a screenshot per finding, the finding, the reference's behaviour, a severity, an enhancement recommendation; the owner aid's format, `candidates-status/index.html`, is the pattern); an **architect triage** per round merges and deduplicates the assessors' findings, pre-grades them and writes one bounded owner sheet, the rest defaulting to tracker rows, and routes a finding that belongs to another surface to that surface's next round or its known items (Decision Log 16); the **owner's verdict** per sheet item (fix, keep, later, with words where the owner has them); an **enhancement wave** (opus workers, one commit per finding, a reviewer round, the named gates); a **re-assessment** by a new set of fresh assessors on the same scenarios plus the fixed items; the **owner's pass**. A finding the owner marks later becomes a tracker row with its constraint. **Spend** is reported per round (the live budget's receipts and the assessors' cost) with no cap. **Live checks of reference behaviour** (the prior art's §1.5, §2.5, §3.4, §4.4) run in the owner's Google, Canva and Figma accounts inside one folder named "MAWS UX check", deleted after the check, with no sharing and no invites (the owner, Decision Log 6); the same rule covers every account the build can reach in a round (a Notion test page and a Drive test folder for L5's deliveries, the owner's Anthropic account for the bench), breaking things is confined to MAWS, and the references are used read-mostly with no destructive act (Decision Log 16); the checks that set a design item's numbers run before that item's plan is written (Docs' margin geometry for B1, Slides' and Canva's typing and first-syllable behaviour for B2, Codex 26.1013's round trip for B3), the rest with the surface's first round. **A surface closes on its own pass;** the order is advisory (documents, then decks, then designs and design systems, dashboards, the library, the shell, the transcript), two surfaces at a time (the owner's verdict load and the compute; Decision Log 15), each round's GUI time in a hold CI-opt schedules (§7). The reference accounts' outward acts and the build's isolation are the protocol's two safety rules.

**4.10 Delegated unknowns.** **S-IME** (P4-B2's spike): the armed editor keeps a first Hangul syllable intact on the real Mac; the content can be cleared on the first keyCode-229 keydown; `preventDefault` suppresses Electron's menu accelerators while armed. **LC-1 to LC-3** (the live checks that set numbers): Docs' margin gap, active shift, deselect behaviour, overflow and collapse widths; what a printable key does on an object-selected box in Slides and Canva and whether the first syllable arrives intact; Codex 26.1013's floating chat round trip. **R-1** (B3): whether an editor instance can be reparented between the panel and a pane without a remount. **R-2** (B4): whether the engine renders the `maws` server's MCP `instructions`, and whether its tools are deferred under `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` with `alwaysLoad` honoured. **R-3** (B4): bm25 over trigrams for Korean and the cost of `read` for 50 hits. **R-4** (P4-A2): the streaming parse's shape for a partially written slide file (parse5 is tolerant; what the provisional element tree looks like mid-file is the child's to find). **R-5** (P4-A2): the provisional bound (1,000 ms per call, the median at 500 ms) against the heaviest cases, confirmed or re-based from the fixture's first run. **R-6** (P4-A1): which artifact kinds the owner's Desktop offers today, and whether engine 2.1.295's transcripts carry `api-request` records (else the parser groups by `requestId`). **R-7** (P4-A2): the wire probe of the launch profile: a session's `init` record under a profile shows the same model and effort as the plain user scope plus exactly the chosen plugins and style, and `disableAllHooks` is the only hook switch. **R-8** (P4-B2): the Google Fonts fetch's egress declaration (which host rule admits `fonts.googleapis.com`, `fonts.gstatic.com` and the google/fonts repository's raw files, and how the owner sees the fetch) and the exact index file the licence is read from.

## 5. Children

Each child carries the tracker rows named in its **Rows** line (the 64 P4-DESIGN rows of the triage, every one homed: 22 on the design children, row 296 on A1, 41 as the surfaces' known items under P4-L); a child closes its rows or re-homes them with a dated line.

### P4-A1 — The parity bench — spike (findings, never a merge of a verdict; the harness lands as a live spec)

- **Purpose:** answer one question before anything about the edit protocol is built: on the same four edits, the same model and effort, is Claude Desktop equally slow from server round trips and output time? §4.2's bench: four tasks, two arms, at least five repetitions per cell with medians, one parser over both apps' engine records, the breakdown per edit, the 1.2 × threshold fixed before the run. First step: which artifact kinds the owner's Desktop offers (R-6).
- **Acceptance:** §2 item 11: `spikes/p4-parity-bench/findings.txt` with the kinds the owner's Desktop offers, the controls verified from the records (model, `output_config.effort`, the cache state, the route, the load; a measurement-only cell pinned to Desktop's setting marked as such), the per-task breakdown for both apps side by side with medians and the spread over at least five repetitions, and the verdict under the 1.2 × threshold per task (a kind Desktop lacks reported without a verdict; a cause outside the levers written as a tracker row); the MAWS arm as `e2e/bench-edit-live.spec.ts` under `MAWS_LIVE=1`, parameterised by task and repetition, reading the X8 journal; the Desktop driver's cua steps and the parser in `spikes/p4-parity-bench/`; tracker row 296 closed on the verdict (Decision Log 10) with its closing line naming the result and the guard as its successor.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: approval; external: Claude Desktop signed in to the owner's account on the chosen host, cua access to it, the relay. blocks: P4-A3 (conditional-on A1's verdict "Desktop clearly faster").
- **Contracts:** X34 (the journal reading the guard shares), X39 (the live-check safety rules apply to Desktop's account use: one project in the owner's Anthropic account named for the bench, deleted after).
- **Design inheritance:** §4.2 [binding on the decision rule], the grounding's §4 (advisory mechanics), §3.
- **Required:** required (the question must be answered); its verdict is a close criterion (Decision Log 17): Desktop faster on a judged task opens P4-A3, and P4 closes only when MAWS's median is within 1.2 times Desktop's on that task.
- **Status:** in-flight: the harness landed 2026-10-20 (merge ae32fe6a8, Decision Log 23; `spikes/p4-parity-bench/findings.txt` §1 the decision rule as amended before any run, §7 the run plan); the record run moves to the Mac mini (Decision Log 45), the decks judged on Cowork's Slides (Decision Log 25); the same executor resumes for the mini's preparation and runs the bench in a GUI hold booked with the gate lane.
- **Rows:** 296 (closes on the verdict).

### P4-A2 — The agent experience — plan

- **Purpose:** the edit shown as it is written (X35: the tool name kept on `content_block_start`, presence from the first delta, the provisional layer under its four rules, drawn by the document editor first and by the slide and design editors after B2); the per-session launch profile (plugins, output style, hooks through `--settings` and `--setting-sources`; the user scope the default; no effort; an X6 amendment); the tool-time guard (X34) as one shared live-spec helper with its heavy-case fixture, the provisional bound (1,000 ms per call, the median at 500 ms) confirmed from the fixture's first run; the guide-duplication defect (`artifact-tools.ts:111`) and pretty-printed results handed to a wave, not fixed here.
- **Acceptance:** §2 items 9 and 10; a live run on the installed engine where an `artifact_edit` on a 200-page document shows presence within one delta of the call's start and its provisional text at the target block before the call completes, the real write replacing it only once the replica has integrated the update `commitSeq` names (no gap, no flash), and a refused batch (a stale `baseRevision` forced by a concurrent edit) withdrawing its layer with nothing in the document or its history; a batch committed while the owner composes keeps its layer until the hold releases the write and then swaps without a gap (the composition-hold e2e extended); the same on a slide publish after B2 lands (gate G2, conditional on B2); Korean composition in a block the agent is streaming into is never disturbed; a session launched with a profile shows in its `init` record the same model and effort as the plain user scope plus exactly the chosen plugins and style (R-7, a protocol-probe fixture) and nothing under a scratch `~/.claude` changed; every paid live spec reads the guard over the named tools, the export and screenshot bound is set from the live specs' timings, and the fixture's heavy cases pass under the bound over the scripted transport.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: approval (the document editor's layer, the guard); P4-B2 (G2, the slide and design editors' layer); W2-A's L94 (the profile's join rule). blocks: P4-L (every surface's "edit seen as it is written").
- **Contracts:** X34 (owner), X35 (owner), X1 and X6 amendments through the ledger, X8 (reader).
- **Design inheritance:** §4.2 [binding on the four rules and the guard's definition], §4.4 (the ARMED and EDIT states the layer draws beneath), P3 §4.4's batch semantics and composition hold [binding there], P3-B's D11 reading as amended by Decision Log 13.
- **Required:** required; G2 conditional on B2's landing.
- **Status:** the first part landed 2026-10-20 (merge 044767eff, Decision Log 28): the document editor's provisional layer, the tool-time guard and fine-grained tool streaming set by MAWS; the launch profile after W2-A's L94 and G2 after B2 stay A2's, each a later dispatch; the launch profile dispatched 2026-10-21 as P4-A2P (Decision Log 52) and landed 2026-10-21 (merge 2b1fa898b, Decision Log 55): one profile in Settings › Engine for every session MAWS launches, by the owner's ruling.
- **Rows:** 298 (presence live; its closing clause rewritten, Decision Log 13), 244 (presence detach race), 270 (the launch profile), 187 (the 24-board publish's host-loop hold, the guard's heaviest fixture case). Row 138 (the block-scoped composition hold) is not A2's: the editor-wide hold stays and rule 2 works with it, so 138 keeps §9's deferral and is the documents surface's known item (Decision Log 16).

### P4-B4 — The agent's tool surface — plan

- **Purpose:** §4.6: `artifact_search` over an FTS5 trigram mirror in the host store with the shared query parser, the schema, matching, order, snippet and `read` as specified, its description and `artifact_list`'s clause; the always-on link instruction by the route the probe picks (R-2); the fragment grammar on `maws-artifact:` links and `show_pane`'s `at`; the X9 and X25 amendments through the ledger with a protocol-probe fixture of the new `tools/list` entry and of the appended instruction in `session.init`; the `alwaysLoad` and deferred-loading probe recorded for A3.
