## 6. Cross-child contracts

Exact names are fixed in the P4 interface ledger (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-19-p4-interface-ledger.md`, opened at approval, one writer, the architect) before any sibling codes against them; the architect amends the charter's §13 at each landing. The numbering continues the charter's global sequence after X32.

- **X33 — the agent tool surface in P4** [owner P4-B4; consumers P4-A2 (the provisional layer's table of which tools stream which `artifact` key and content field), P4-A3 if it runs (publish `edits`, the guides, `alwaysLoad`), P4-L's briefs]. **Binding because** the `maws` server's names, descriptions, schemas and refusals are the owner's skills' interface (X9's rule: they change only by a dated line) and three children touch them in one phase. The content: `artifact_search`'s name, schema, description, annotation and refusals as §4.6 states them; the `artifact_list` clause; the always-on link instruction's text and route; the fragment grammar `#v<n>`, `#tab=`, `#block=`, `#slide=` on `maws-artifact:` links and `show_pane`'s `at`; the host store's FTS mirror table (an X23 ledger row); a protocol-probe fixture per wire change (D-1). X9 and X25 amended through the ledger; `artifact_create` and the existing tools keep their names.
- **X34 — the tool-time guard** [owner P4-A2; binds every paid live spec (`e2e/*-live.spec.ts`), P4-A1's harness, P4-L's rounds, P4-F's close]. **Binding because** it is the close's one numeric criterion on MAWS's own performance and every later landing's live spec must read it the same way. The content: per guarded call (the thirteen store tools named in §4.2; `artifact_export` and `artifact_screenshot` under a bound of their own, provisional from the live specs' timings), MAWS execution is the span in the launch's X8 journal from the `mcp_message` frame carrying `tools/call` in to the `control_response` frame out, read by one helper in `test/live/support/`; a spec fails when any call exceeds the bound or the spec's median exceeds half of it; the bound is a named constant beside its grounding (the sitting's 0.02 to 0.19 s; the heavy fixture's numbers), 1,000 ms per call and the spec's median at 500 ms, provisional, confirmed from the heavy fixture's first run at A2 (Decision Log 15); the heavy-case fixture (a 24-board design publish, a 100-slide deck publish, a 200-page document batch) drives the tools over the wire through the conformance scripted transport so the journal carries the frames, and runs with the guard on every gate that runs live specs. The guard measures MAWS, never the model or the server (the holds of §3 are excluded by construction).
- **X35 — the editors' provisional layer** [owner P4-A2; consumers P4-B1 (the document editor, whose margin must ignore it), P4-B2 (the slide and design editors, whose stage states it draws beneath), P3-E's dashboard view]. **Binding because** one host-side stream is drawn by three editors and the owner's acceptance of a revealed-then-withdrawn edit rests on its rules. The content: the host's `artifact.provisional` event per open document port `{toolUseId, appSessionId, artifactId, kind, target: {block?} | {paths?} | {view?}, text, state: 'streaming' | 'committed' | 'dropped', commitSeq?}` from the first delta that parses the `artifact` key, batched at about 16 ms; the four rules of §4.2 (never a Y write, its growth a `layoutKey` change coalesced per animation frame; withdrawn only when the renderer's replica has integrated the update `commitSeq` names, the withdrawal buffered with the held Y frames under the composition hold; withdrawn without trace on refusal; never inside a block the human is composing in and never stealing focus or selection); presence published from the first delta with the same actor and colour as the committed write; the tool name kept on `content_block_start` (an additive X1 amendment); the layer changes no `layoutKey`, version, provenance or history. Amends the sitting item D11 as wave S read it (Decision Log 13).
- **X36 — the shared comment card** [owner P4-B1; consumer P4-B2; the All comments panel and the icon-rail overlay as further hosts]. **Binding because** the owner's "the same comment everywhere" rests on one component two surfaces draw. The content: `ThreadCard` in `src/artifacts/` with the compacted inactive form (the first message and the two latest replies; no quoted passage), the active form (the reply field focused on activation), the keys (R, E, J, K, U, Escape), the draft rule (Escape on a non-empty draft keeps it; one draft per artifact; a hidden card keeps its draft), the resolve and reopen actions, the author chip from the actor table; placement outside the card (the margin stack; the pure `placeCard` moved under `src/artifacts/comments/placement.ts` for the popover and the compose card, the renderer re-exporting it). An X21 amendment through the ledger (the card component; the placement kinds gain `margin` and `pin`).
- **X37 — the main-area float** [owner P4-B3; consumers P3-G's floating card and pill, the browser pane's reserved column, every editor that reads the card's rect, the layout store]. **Binding because** two float states (the panel's and the split's) share one card and one keymap. The content: the split's `floating` state beside `splitRatio` (which a float never writes), `cardSession` as the collapsed pane's session, the pixel minimums `S_min = A_min = 320` and the threshold `S_min / 2` as named constants, both floating cards at the bottom right of their regions (the panel card aligned by B3 if its corner differs; Decision Log 15), one card at a time (⌥⌘B docks the split card first), the resize rule (never floats or docks), relaunch (the card open), the card's rect reservation API the editors may read (the browser column's shape), `data-markup-occluder` on the card layer; an X5 note (the host sees the split's float as the panel's), an X30 amendment, an X15 slot if a layout version is taken.
- **X38 — cross-surface tab transfer** [owner P4-B3; consumers P3-L's Artifacts tool, the main tab strip and `DropEdge`, every editor (the state carry), the keymap]. **Binding because** two surfaces and every editor must agree on what a drop means. The content: the drag types `application/x-maws-tab` (an artifact main tab) and `application/x-maws-panel-tab {toolId, tabId}`; `dragging.source: 'main' | 'panel'`; move only, no copy by drag (the owner); a drop on a surface already showing the artifact activates it and closes the source, an unsent draft in the source carried to the activated view (X36's one-draft rule); a session tab never accepted by the panel; the refusal reason shared by the drop, "Show in panel", "Move to tab" and "Move to main area"; the state carry per kind (scroll, selection, every edit exactly once, drafts; a session's process) or a refusal; the invariants INV-1 to INV-6; the actions registered without default chords; the pinned Artifacts tool's record in its own persisted state (row 290's constraint) or a layout version through X15.
- **X39 — the live user test protocol** [owner P4-L; binds every child whose landing a surface assesses, P4-W's enhancement waves, P4-A1's and the live checks' account use, P4-F]. **Binding because** the close is seven passes under one definition. The content: the host named per round at dispatch; the dedicated build with its own MAWS user data, its sessions on the owner's user scope like any MAWS session (the owner's artifacts only with the owner's go); fresh frontier assessors per round and a new set for the re-assessment; the brief's shape (the surface's real scenarios, "every corner", "try to break it" on MAWS only, the reference comparison read-mostly, the known-items list); the picture page's format; the architect's triage per round (merge, dedup, pre-grade; one bounded owner sheet, the rest as rows; findings of another surface routed to it); the owner's verdict sheet (fix, keep, later); the enhancement wave under the wave rules; the pass in the owner's words; the spend reported per round with no cap; the outward-act rule over every account the build can reach: the owner's Google, Canva and Figma accounts inside one folder "MAWS UX check", a Notion test page and a Drive test folder for L5, the owner's Anthropic account inside one project named for the bench (the architect names it to the owner), each deleted after, no sharing, no invites, no destructive act in a reference; a surface closes on its own pass. Written to outlive this unit as the UX assessment protocol later phases reuse; promoted to the charter at P4-F.
- **Amendments through the ledger:** X1 (the tool name on `content_block_start`; additive), X5 (the split's float), X6 (the launch profile: `--settings` keys on every launch, one app-wide profile since Decision Log 55; the user scope the default), X9 and X25 (`artifact_search`, the link instruction, `show_pane`'s `at`; A3's publish `edits` if it runs), X15 (a layout version, a settings version for the profile and the comment view state), X18 (the always-on instruction, if routed through the owner channel), X21 (the card component), X23 (the host's FTS mirror; `versionId` on `artifacts.assets` from W1-D), X30 (the two cards), X32 (Expand and Send to as header actions). X15 stands: slots in landing order, the architect renumbers at each landing.

## 7. Ordering and dependency map

- **Wave 0, at approval:** P4-W's W1 holds the four worker slots (P1 A-13); the P4 interface ledger opens with X36's and X37's names. **The slot order** (Decision Log 16): as each W1 batch lands its slot goes to the next child in this order, each still under its own blocked-by: A1's harness (Desktop's kinds confirmed first), B2's IME spike, A2 (the guard and the document editor's layer), B2's plan (after L281), B1 (LC-1 first or on Liveblocks' numbers), B3 (after W1-A), B4 (after W1-D), B5. **The mini calendar rule:** every GUI-taking job (the bench's Desktop runs, the IME spike when it needs the real input source, the live checks LC-1 to LC-3, every L round) runs in a hold CI-opt schedules outside landing windows (gate-offload Decision Log 78's procedure), one GUI hold at a time; a cua-driven check on the MacBook takes the owner's GUI and runs only with the owner's go.
- **Wave 1:** P4-B1 after LC-1 (or on Liveblocks' numbers, re-based later); P4-B2's plan after its spike and W1-C's L281; P4-B3 after W1-A; P4-B4 after W1-D; W2 after W1 (W2-A's L94 before A2's profile; W2-D's L213 before B1 and B2 implement, advisory).
- **Wave 2:** P4-A2's G2 (the slide and design editors' layer) after B2; P4-A2's profile after L94; W3 after W2 (W3-B's L242 after B2); WR when the owner's responses arrive; P4-A3 if A1's verdict calls it, after A1 and W1-D.
- **Wave 3:** P4-L's cut and its surfaces, each round 1 when that surface's items and waves have landed and the 80 responses are in; documents first, then decks, designs and design systems, dashboards, the library, the shell, the transcript (advisory order); two surfaces at a time.
- **Wave 4:** P4-F after every surface's pass.
- Forced: W1-D → B4; W1-C's L281 → B2's implementation; W1-A → B3; B2's spike → B2's plan; B1's X36 names → B2's popover; B2 → A2's G2; L94 → A2's profile; A1 → A3 (conditional); A2, B1 to B5, W1 to W3, WR → L; L → F. At most four workers at once (P1 A-13), the slot script, one e2e group at a time on the mini, the load rule, class 1a on the pool (gate-offload §3.5). Landings follow P3's pattern: the throwaway-worktree master gate, the records, the push, `--no-ff`.

## 8. Risks and mitigations

- **The first Hangul syllable.** The armed editor is the only arrangement that keeps it, and the platform may refuse the clear-on-229 step. Mitigation: B2's spike before its plan, on the real Mac through cua; the degraded "append" verdict and the Slides variant as named fallbacks; the recorded trace as the simulated cases' fixture.
- **The margin on the typing path.** Row 235's p95 already sits at the bound; a margin that re-lays on every edit would break it. Mitigation: re-layout only on `layoutKey`; B1 measures the 200-page workload with the margin open before its tick; A2's layer changes no `layoutKey`.
- **A withdrawn edit confuses.** A provisional layer the host then refuses is seen and vanishes. Mitigation: X35's rules (labelled provisional, in the agent's colour, withdrawn in one frame with the refusal in the transcript); the owner chose it knowing this (Decision Log 6).
- **The split card over the editor's chrome.** A bottom-right card covers a deck's inspector. Mitigation: X37's rect reservation the editors may read; the browser column as the proven pattern; the loop's decks round tests it.
- **Reparenting an editor.** If an instance cannot move without a remount, every carried state is explicit work. Mitigation: R-1 first; per-kind transfer; S4 (undo across a move) may invert into a logged row.
- **The parity bench's controls.** Desktop's gates, cache states and effort can drift between arms. Mitigation: the first step reads the gates from `init`; every control verified from the records; the rule stated before the run; the verdict reported with its controls.
- **The loop's cost and the owner's time.** No cap, seven surfaces, two assessments each. Mitigation: spend reported per round; surfaces close independently; the known-items list stops re-reporting; the owner's verdict sheet is short (fix, keep, later).
- **Outward acts in the owner's accounts.** Live checks and the bench create documents at Google, Canva, Figma and Anthropic. Mitigation: X39's one folder, deleted after, no sharing, no invites; the checks named in advance; the owner's go on the folder.
- **The guard's bound.** Set too tight it fails on the mini's load; too loose it guards nothing. Mitigation: the bound set from the heavy fixture's first run on both hosts with the load recorded; the spec's median at half the bound catches drift before the maximum does.
- **Three contract owners touching the tool surface.** B4, A2 and A3 (if it runs) all change what the model sees. Mitigation: X33 with one owner; a fixture per wire change; A3's changes through B4's ledger rows.
- **A regression on P3's acceptance.** Every design item sits inside a P3 editor. Mitigation: §1's rule (a tripped P3 e2e is a regression to fix, never an expectation to change without a dated line); the P3 groups in every landing gate.

## 9. Deferred and out of scope

**Deferred (may return in P4 through A3, or later).** The three edit-protocol levers, deferred by Decision Log 8 with the grounding's evidence and returned only by A1's verdict (Decision Log 10): **patch publish for decks and designs** (the sitting's whole-file publishes wrote 1,368 to 3,326 output tokens for a date, a font and a line; Desktop's server-hosted types also publish whole files, so MAWS is at parity on the protocol, and 2.1.300's `edits` pattern is the shape if it returns); **two requests per edit** (the guides' read-back and the separate reply cost 1.5 to 2.7 s of first byte and a hold each; Desktop pays 2 to 6 requests on the same engine); **the context diet** (compact results; per-artifact sessions; the duplicated guide text is a wave defect regardless). Also deferred: the effort decision and an edit-session effort (the owner: no effort adjustment); the deck-hold probe (a server-side hold MAWS cannot remove; report to Anthropic if it reproduces, outside P4); the `mcp__maws__*` allow rule (it would let an edit land during a hold but takes the server classifier out of the gate for MAWS's own tools; needs a security reading); the full comparison harness of the grounding's §4 (nine tasks, six arms, five repetitions) beyond A1's four tasks; native PPTX charts and Morph (row 177, the owner: later, when decks go to PowerPoint regularly); deck suggestions with per-slide accept and reject (row 174; A3's if it runs); a copy by drag (the owner: move only; P3-L Decision Log 8's replicas stay reachable from the list); embeddings for `artifact_search` (after R-3's measured misses); element-level accessibility roles on the canvas if B2 cannot bound them; a chord for Expand (registered without a default); a composition hold scoped to the composing block (P3's deferral; row 138 stays deferred, the editor-wide hold stands and X35's rule 2 works with it; the documents surface's known item); the panel divider's convergence on the live, reversible rule (logged as debt); the owner's hands rows (181 the presenter and the PPTX variants, into the decks surface's round; 204 ClaudeUsage on the mini, the gate lane's). **P5's seed** (the owner, 2026-10-19): sharing, simultaneous editing by other people, a cloud and web-hosted MAWS, the teachers product, design systems distributed to others; the P3 spec's §9 keeps the rest of its list.

**Out of P4's scope by bin (not deferred, not P4's):** the 87 OTHER-LANE rows (the desk's 33, the cua and in-app-browser lane's 32, the gate lane's 12, the Electron and engine bumps' 7, the voice lane's 2, the next settings version's 1) and the 20 HOLD rows (upstream re-pins, a recorded case, a live probe on the pinned engine, a sitting or a trace), each by the triage's bin; HWP and HWPX export (row 156, the owner: drop; closed).

**Explicitly out of scope (standing exclusions).** claude.ai's Docs, Artifacts and Cowork services as a store (P1 P-4); patching the engine (D-1); MAWS writing under `~/.claude` or `~/.claude.json` (D-1; the launch profile goes through the launch's options, X6); Windows and Linux (D-11); a second engine (D-7); the browser's top chrome (the cua session's); a rewrite of P3's store, editors or protocol (P3 §4.10); a close on agents' opinions alone (§1); a second main split (D-8).

## 11. Surprises and discoveries

- 2026-10-19 (grounding, the bench): the sitting's "14 s `artifact_publish`" and "11 s `artifact_reply`" were server-side holds after the model finished the call, not MAWS time (MAWS's execution 0.02 to 0.19 s); tracker row 296's routing ("the deck's publish and reply first on the tool-time side") pointed at the wrong place. Found by joining the transcript, the X8 journal and the relay log (`research/p4-edit-speed-grounding.md` §2.3).
- 2026-10-19 (grounding, the bench): every guide arrives twice (`artifact_describe_type` never marks a kind as guided), about 82K tokens of the note session's 527K to 555K; the partial tool input (9,465 deltas) reaches MAWS and is used only by the transcript's live row; MAWS passes no model or effort, the owner's `settings.json` does.
- 2026-10-19 (grounding, the bench): Claude Desktop has no faster edit primitive on the same engine; its server-hosted types publish whole files and its same-engine artifacts appear only after the call; what feels fast is claude.ai's chat artifacts on a different engine.
- 2026-10-19 (the prior art): Google Slides shows a deck's comments as cards beside the slide and documents no on-canvas pin; the pin precedent is Figma and Canva, so the plan's "Slides' pins" was wrong. No product floats a chat on a width breakpoint; three implementations (VS Code, Codex, Claude Desktop) collapse a view at half its minimum, live and reversibly. No product reaches grouped text in one double-click; the one-gesture reach is MAWS's own. The first Hangul syllable needs an editor focused before the first key (Chromium's input context and AppKit's 100 ms timer; wave S F9 had already excluded Hangul for this reason). Main's library search is one phrase ordered by recency with a 64-token snippet, so mirroring it would inherit all three weaknesses. Cursor switched its semantic codebase search off in July 2026.
- 2026-10-19 (the prior art): the Space half of obs 6 had already landed in wave S F5; owner-observations §6 is stale on it. `research/SOURCES.md` names a Codex build one behind the one on this machine (`chatgpt-26.1013.31256`, which dropped the gated composer-only overlay).
- 2026-10-19 (the triage): 16 rows record their own closure or describe code that is already otherwise (L273's premise false: the history list does refresh on `artifacts.changed`); two pairs are one defect; ten rows name landed owners; eight cite wrong paths or causes. The WAVE queue is three waves, not one.
- 2026-10-19 (the owner): the worry was MAWS's own tool performance; slowness from inference is accepted and effort is not to be tuned (Decision Log 8), then the Desktop comparison was wanted after all as one bounded parity question (Decision Log 10).

## 12. Decision log

1. **2026-10-19 (owner), round 1, the phase.** P4 is the smoothing phase; sharing, simultaneous editing and a web-hosted MAWS move to P5 (the charter amended at 6db299a01). Scope: every daily surface, the artifact editors and the session-supervising surfaces (the sidebar, the transcript, tabs, the panel, notifications). The close: the owner's declaration plus bench targets (the same model and effort on an idle host), plus an agent-run adversarial UX assessment loop through cua_repl (assessors, assessments with enhancement recommendations, the owner's review and verdict, an enhancement wave, another assessment pass and the owner's pass); "we don't have to declare the end of P4 … UX improvement is a hill climb that is nearly endless", so P4 ends at "done to what extent" with further jobs carried to later waves and phases; the sitting's cua-driven agent gave many good UX suggestions, do the same. Rejected: a close on the declaration alone; a fixed scope list.
2. **2026-10-19 (owner), round 1, slowness.** Both the total time and the time to the first visible edit are felt; benchmark directly to decide inference against tool performance. (Superseded in part by 8 and 10.)
3. **2026-10-19 (owner), round 2.** The host for assessments and the bench: decide at the time, wherever compute is free. Cost cap: none; "the key is that the models don't just converse but use every corner of the product in real scenarios, as a live user test". The extent: the named design-item list lands, the bench targets are met and each surface passes (assessment, verdict, enhancement wave, re-assessment pass and the owner's pass); the rest is carried forward. The first assessment is not now: the 80 candidates already hold many current defects; fix the discussed items to a degree, the owner gives responses on the 80 soon, those land, and with all known defects addressed round 1 runs at the frontier. Rejected: an assessment round before the known items land.
4. **2026-10-19 (owner), the triage's OWNER rows.** L133 UI language: English throughout (dates, relative ages); Korean UI possible later; a wave item (W2-D's sweep, about 24 suites' expectations). L156 HWP and HWPX export: drop; the row closes. L176 Google Fonts: fetch once into the deck's assets, OFL only, offline after; a P4 smoothing item (B2). L177 native PPTX charts and Morph: later, when decks go to PowerPoint regularly. Rejected: system-locale ages inside English copy (D2's rule reversed for the daily surfaces).
5. **2026-10-19 (architect).** L32's catalogue-key residue ruled final unless the owner objects (told ~20:3xZ). The split card's corner: bottom right, the owner's words in obs 4. Escape on a non-empty comment draft keeps the draft (Word desktop's rule; Figma's volatile drafts are the complaint to avoid; MAWS's `placement.ts` already keeps a hidden card's draft); a deliberate departure from Docs, recorded for the owner. Rejected: Docs' cancel-on-Escape.
6. **2026-10-19 (owner), the five questions (~20:3xZ).** The click model: Canva's select-first (over Slides' caret on the first click; the fallback stays named in §4.4). The streaming display of agent edits: yes, a provisional layer committed at the call's completion (amends D11 as wave S read it; X35). The comment margin: reserved when the window is wide (the page moves left when the first thread appears). Tab drag: move only; a drop on a surface already showing the artifact activates it. Live checks: in the owner's Google, Canva and Figma accounts inside one test folder "MAWS UX check", deleted after, no sharing or invites. Rejected: Slides' caret-first as the default; Option-copy by drag; a test account for the live checks.
7. **2026-10-19 (architect), the grounding's correction.** Tracker row 296's routing is wrong: the deck's 14 s and 11 s were server-side holds after the call was written, MAWS's execution 0.02 to 0.19 s; the tool-time side is the guide-duplication defect, compact results and `alwaysLoad`; the model-time side is publish patches, the read-back and reply round trips, session size and the streamed display. The correction is recorded here and in the row's closing line (10).
8. **2026-10-19 (owner, 20:36Z), track A narrowed.** Verbatim: "걱정한건 툴 자체의 성능이라 툴 자체 성능 이외의 것들로 인해 속도가 느린거라면 그런것들은 괜찮다. effort 조정 이런건 필요없음." (the worry was MAWS's own tool performance; slowness from anything else is fine; no effort adjustment needed). Applied: the Desktop comparison benchmark dropped as a child and as a close criterion (restored in bounded form by 10); the effort decision, the edit-session effort profile and the deck-hold probe dropped; patch publish and the two-requests-per-edit lever deferred with the grounding's evidence (Desktop's server-hosted types also publish whole files, so MAWS is at parity); the streaming display, the launch profile without effort and a tool-time guard kept in one "agent experience" child (A2); the duplicated guide text a wave defect; the close's bench criterion "MAWS tool time under the guard's bound"; the assessment loop's surfaces seven (the felt edit speed dropped as a surface). Rejected: a close criterion on inference time.
9. **2026-10-19 (architect), the guard as row 296's successor.** The paid live specs read each `artifact_*` call's MAWS execution from the X8 journal and fail above a bound (X34); the finding of 7 stays true by construction. The guide defect (`artifact-tools.ts:111`) and pretty-printed results go to a wave. (Row 296's closure moved to 10.)
10. **2026-10-19 (owner, 20:41Z; the shape the architect's), the parity bench restored.** The owner, verbatim: "Claude Desktop 비교 벤치 --  이것도 해보는 게 어떨까 싶다. 만약에 터드 데스크탑에서도 서버 왕복 시간 그리고 출원 시간 때문에 긴 거라면 우리는 문제가 없는 것. 그러나 그게 아니라면 다른 문제가 있다는 뜻이기 때문에 해볼 가치가 있다고 본다 어떻게 생각하나?" ("터드 데스크탑" is Claude Desktop and "출원 시간" output time, as dictated). The architect agreed and proposed the shape, as child A1: four tasks (a one-word document fix, a paragraph rewrite, a one-slide date change, adding a slide), MAWS in a fresh session against Desktop on equivalent artifacts, the same model and effort (the owner's usual setting; no effort tuning), three repetitions each (superseded by Decision Log 16.2: at least five, medians), timed from both apps' engine records by one parser; the first step confirms which artifact kinds the owner's Desktop offers; the breakdown per edit (requests, input tokens, first-byte wait, generation time and output tokens, server hold, tool execution); the decision rule stated before the run: within about 20 % on the same task with similar requests and tokens is parity (superseded by Decision Log 16.2's single 1.2 times threshold) and closes the question; Desktop clearly faster means the breakdown names the cause and the matching deferred levers (patch publish, the request diet, the context diet) return to P4 as the edit-protocol child A3; the host wherever compute is free, Desktop driven through cua with the timing from the engine records; not a close criterion by itself (the architect's framing; the owner rules on it in the approval ask). **Tracker row 296 closes on the parity result**, not at the spec's commit; its closing line, to be written at A1's landing: "Closed <date> on P4-A1's parity verdict (`spikes/p4-parity-bench/findings.txt`): <parity within N % | Desktop faster by N %, cause: …>; the sitting's 14 s and 11 s were server-side holds, MAWS's execution 0.02 to 0.19 s (P4 Decision Log 7); the standing successor is the tool-time guard X34 in every paid live spec; <A3 dispatched | no edit-protocol child>." Rejected: closing the row at the spec's commit on the grounding alone.

Rejected: one child per surface at this level (the loop's surfaces are L's cut); a separate "design residue" child for the 40 rows (they are the surfaces' known items and the owner's verdicts decide them).

16. **2026-10-19 (architect), the adversarial review's sixteen findings** (`docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4/spec-review.md`), each resolved in place: (1) X35's commit: the layer is withdrawn only when the update carrying the call's identity integrates in the renderer (`committed` carries `commitSeq`; the composition hold buffers the withdrawal with the Y frames it holds), so nothing vanishes during a composition and nothing flashes between two messages; row 138 resolved against §9: the editor-wide hold stands, 138 stays deferred and leaves A2 for the documents surface's known items. (2) The parity bench: at least five repetitions per cell with medians and the spread; one threshold fixed beforehand (MAWS's median send-to-edit-landed above 1.2 × Desktop's means Desktop is faster, otherwise parity; no middle band); a cell Desktop cannot pin to the owner's setting (Cowork for the deck kinds) pins MAWS's arm to Desktop's actual setting as a measurement-only condition, recorded as such; a kind Desktop lacks reports components without a verdict; a cause outside the deferred levers becomes a tracker row, never A3; Decision Log 10's record of the owner's words stands. (8) The provisional layer may change block height; B1's margin re-layout includes it, coalesced to one per animation frame, and B1's keystroke measurement includes a streaming layer. 27. **2026-10-20 (the architect, at the W1 follow-up's landing, 02:50Z, merge 7ad99ceb0), W1's follow-up landed and W1 is accepted; the freed slot to LC-1, then W2-A.** The follow-up (one opus worker in an isolated worktree, dispatched 2026-10-20 00:54Z on 986871726, Decision Log 22) found that neither red was a W1 regression: both cases failed at the same rate at the pre-W1 base 94f6463ea as on master (the link case alone 9 of 40 at the base against 7 of 40 on master, the whole document spec 2 of 6 on each; the bridge case alone 3 of 30 against 1 of 30), and the link case had only one gate run before W1, so "green in every pre-W1 transcript" rested on that one run. Causes, each traced with instrumented runs on the mini: (1) `e2e/document-editor.spec.ts:537`: prosemirror-view's focus handler writes its stored selection back into the DOM 20 ms after the editor takes focus when the DOM differs from what it last read; the harness's `placeCaret` resolved at once, and a busy renderer ran that timer late, in the middle of the Shift+ArrowLeft presses, so presses were lost (selection writes traced in run 39353031156); the product is sound, a person would have to extend a selection by keyboard within about 20 ms of focusing; the fix 377d71ace makes `placeCaret` wait a 20 ms timer of its own set after ProseMirror's, with `caret.test.ts` red before it. (2) `e2e/deliverables.spec.ts:216`: in every failure the app's own document received the real pointer events aimed at `IFRAME#e2-frame` and the out-of-process frame received nothing, Chromium not yet routing input into the just-loaded frame (in 16 of 63 runs the first real move went to the app's document and the second, about 130 ms later, reached the frame); the product is sound, a click in that gap lands on the iframe element and picking stays on; the fix 9b64263be moves the real pointer a pixel back and forth until the overlay is up, then clicks. Stable: run 39356553612 on the head plus repeat flags only (`e2e:document` 8 times 136 of 136, the link case alone 40 of 40; `e2e:deliverables` 5 times 60 of 60, the bridge case alone 40 of 40). The gate under Decision Log 56: run 39355527590 on 377d71ace (static, unit, probes, `e2e:document`, `e2e:deliverables`), every job green. Review: reviewer-medium on opus, correct with no material findings (its one note, a window without OS focus delaying the focus event, set aside because Playwright emulates focus there). Under the gate-offload spec's Decision Log 57, master's moves since 986871726 share no path with the branch's three files and the merge-tree dry run's tree 66e2fbb7b is the merge's, so the landing took no further gate. W1's acceptance: the combined tree 32bebd35f's whole class-1 set (run 39342835987) was green on 37 jobs with packaged skipped by plan and red only on these two cases, now shown to be test races present before W1 and fixed, so W1 is accepted; its ledger's Outcome is written. Two residue rows added (the same one-move overlay shape in the comment-mode and p1-live cases; a launch-time window resize timing out in local MacBook repeats). The follow-up's slot goes first to LC-1, run by one bounded worker in a tab of the owner's Chrome profile `main` through the Playwright extension under Decision Log 25 on B1's measurement list (B1's plan, e57a0fb91), and then to W2-A (L94 lands before A2's profile); W2's other batches, B4 and B5 take slots as A2, B1 and B3 land, and A1's record run takes the first slot freed after the time the owner names.

One rule, `reconcileFold` in `src/renderer/src/stores/layout.ts`, runs on every change of `main` or `regions` and once at hydration: when the marked float ends by any path (Restore split, the card's Dock, ⌥⌘B docking the split card, A's last tab closing, S's tab closing or moving, the session removed) while the panel is still folded, the panel opens docked; when the panel opens by any path while expanded (its toggle, Maximize, Float, a tool or an attach revealing it), the mark drops and leaving Expand leaves the panel as the owner set it, so reopened then closed stays closed, maximized stays maximized and opened then floated stays floating. 46. **2026-10-21 (the architect, 02:23Z), mid-turn messages to the human go through a message tool, and MAWS's side is routed to P4-R7.** doperpowers-88 reported (SSFSKIM/doperpowers#304; its spec `docs/doperpowers/specs/2026-10-20-to-human-message-tool-design.md`, branch `to-human-message-tool` of SSFSKIM/doperpowers) that on `claude-opus-5-5` and `claude-fable-5-1`, text the model writes before a tool call in the same response comes back from the API as a progress-update thinking block: a paraphrase without its `<message>` tags, empty unless the request sets display `updates`. On this Mac about half the mid-turn messages since 2026-10-02 were lost to every reader of text blocks, MAWS's to-human parser (`src/shared/owner/to-human-parser.ts`) among them, and some of them asked the owner to act. The owner approved the doperpowers message tool on 2026-10-20. The cross-repo contract: the plugin's `mcp__to-human__message` and MAWS's own `mcp__maws__message`, input `{text}` in Markdown, result `sent`, an accepted call read as a message at its place in the transcript. The plugin style declares contract 6, the message-tool shape MAWS already reads (`styleShape`), so until MAWS's side lands MAWS appends its supplement 1 and keeps today's behaviour; 7 is MAWS's next full contract; the plugin's tool does not register when MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN is set. P4-R7 (brief grain, opus) builds MAWS's side after a probe on MAWS's own wire (D-1): the message tool beside `mcp__maws__ask`; the parser and every reader of to-human parts taking the tool's calls under both names; a shape boundary at 6 with supplement 2 and the full contract 7; and a small live eval of the wording. The desk lane builds the desk's side. The reason is daily use: MAWS's report is where the owner reads their sessions, and a lost mid-turn message is often the one that asks them to act.

In MAWS the plugin's tool is another tool: MAWS launches every session with its own `maws` server (an SDK server the engine host answers, declared at initialize), supplement 2 tells the model to use `mcp__maws__message`, and under MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN the plugin does not register its tool (doperpowers #312, merge 3e591393, its style declaring 6), so a plugin call in a session MAWS shows comes only from a session whose lane was off or that began outside MAWS, and reading it as a message would hide that. 50. **2026-10-21 (the architect, at P4-R7y's landing, 07:45Z, merge e9b6b6525), the owner's SessionStart alias: a lane-on MAWS session is told at every start that `mcp__to-human__message` is `mcp__maws__message`, and P4-R7y landed it.** The owner (2026-10-21, about 06:40Z): a SessionStart hook in every MAWS session injects "mcp__to-human__message is mcp__maws__message tool in this session". The owner's style names the plugin's tool, and supplement 2 already tells the model to use MAWS's, but an appended prompt can be missing or stale (a resumed session keeps the prompt it recorded until the launch decision turns the snapshot off, and a snapshot defers a new append to the next compaction), while a SessionStart hook speaks at every start. The architect ruled the hook goes in only while the session's lane is on, the condition under which the launch advertises MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN and the plugin's tool is absent, since with the lane off the plugin's tool is real and the statement would be false. The probe (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-p4-r7y-session-start.txt`, 13 fixtures `fixtures/p4/p4-r7y-*.jsonl`, engine 2.1.304 on MAWS's launch line, initialize and lane-on `--settings`): a host hook callback registered through `initialize.hooks` misses the first start of every launch (startup, resume and fork; the engine runs SessionStart hooks before it reads `initialize`) and reaches only /compact and /clear, while a command hook in the `--settings` flag JSON reaches every start (startup, /compact, /clear, a resume and a second resume with one copy, a fork with source `fork`), each next request carrying the text once as a system message; on a real model (Haiku 5.5 through the relay) a new session without the hook named no tool for `mcp__to-human__message`, the same session resumed with the hook and a new session with it named `mcp__maws__message`; 2.1.306 gave the same table on the stub runs; about US$0.013 spent of the US$0.25 allowed. So a start-time context goes through the `--settings` hooks, never `HostHookRegistry`. The change: `src/shared/owner/contract.ts` gains `PLUGIN_MESSAGE_TOOL`, `MESSAGE_TOOL_ALIAS` (the text: "In this session `mcp__to-human__message` is the `mcp__maws__message` tool: wherever your instructions name `mcp__to-human__message`, call `mcp__maws__message`, which takes the same input.") and `messageToolHookGroup()`, one SessionStart group with no matcher running `/usr/bin/printf %s <answer JSON>` in exec form (no shell, none of the owner's shell configuration); `withOwnerChannel` (`src/main/sessions/launch-mapping.ts`) adds it to `extraSettings.hooks.SessionStart` under exactly the condition that sets MAWS_NATIVE_TO_HUMAN, keeping caller hooks, never doubling on a retried spec and removing it with the lane off; the trust bootstrap carries none; supplement 2 unchanged. A SessionStart hook is now MAWS-owned in every lane-on session: one short process per start, under the 600 s floor that holds `initialize` for any SessionStart hook, drawing nothing in the transcript on success. A2's per-session launch profile, whose X6 amendment reaches hooks only through `disableAllHooks`, keeps this group when it disables hooks, or the alias falls back to supplement 2 alone. The probe (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-p4-r8-mcp-servers.txt`, fixtures `fixtures/p4/p4-r8-owner.jsonl` and `p4-r8-scratch.jsonl`; engine 2.1.306, the realpath of `~/.local/bin/claude` that MAWS's sessions resolve with no binary named in Settings; MAWS's launch line, environment, gateway route and initialize; no prompt, nothing spent, no dialog): a `--mcp-config` server reads `scope: "dynamic"`; the owner's login is reused (in the owner's config `notion` connected in 552 ms with 65 tools, the engine keying OAuth tokens by name, type, URL and headers, never by scope; `~/.claude.json` unchanged), so the brief's sign-in from MAWS was left out; a server also named in user, local and project scope is listed once with the `--mcp-config` URL, the three shadowed entries never contacted, so MAWS keeps the engine's precedence; a server needing auth reads `needs-auth` with no tools, and `mcp_authenticate` answers an `authUrl` with the engine's own localhost callback, opening no browser; a `--mcp-config` failing validation as a whole makes the engine exit, so MAWS validates every entry before a launch passes it. The change: settings version 16 adds `engine.mcpServers` (entries `{name, type: 'http', url}`, at most 32, names unique and following the engine's rule, `maws`, the Object.prototype names and the 2.1.304 engine's reserved names refused, the URL http or https and refused rather than trimmed; a version 15 file migrates with an empty list) in `src/shared/engine/app-mcp-servers.ts`; `appMcpConfig` is the one function a launch reads them through, over C10's live settings seam; new, fork, side thread and resume pass one `--mcp-config` and an empty list none; the trust bootstrap none; `--strict-mcp-config` stays off; Settings › Engine › MCP servers (`McpServersSettings.tsx`) adds and removes entries, explains a refusal beside the fields, waits for the store's written list before the next edit, and says a change applies at each session's next launch. Settings version 16 had been reserved for A2's profile default (the interface ledger's §6), which now takes 17. Route D (P3-HD) attests only user- and local-scope entries, so a Notion connector bound in an artifact is engine-only (route E, while an engine is live) until route D also attests MAWS's own setting, the architect's note for the connectors lane. 61. **2026-10-21 (the architect, at the integration landing, 19:43Z, merge b0db20b35), P4-R9 landed with the integration: the deck editor's inspector fields and the text box's mark bar follow every write as the app ships, and the compiled coverage of the artifact suites has no exclusion.** Dispatched 2026-10-21 at 11:14Z (a64ee1358f1a8d0e2, opus, worktree, branch `p4-r9` off W2-D's 5101ae6d3) from W2-D's L213 residue: three deck-editor suites failed only under the React Compiler, with which the app is built. The compiler memoizes on what a computation reads and ignores the dependency list: the Inspector's `picked` was keyed on `doc, selection, slideId`, and `doc` is the same Yjs object at every update, so its fields kept the values they opened with until the selection changed; the mark bar read `editor.isActive` in render, cached on the one editor object. The fixes (6d4821ff9, tests written first, red under `artifacts-dom-compiled` and green under `artifacts-dom` before): `pickedAt(doc, slideId, selection, version)` with the version from a shared `useDocVersion(session)` on `useSyncExternalStore`, and the bar selecting its marks through `@tiptap/react`'s `useEditorState`; `vitest.config.ts` drops the exclusion. The sweep (`eslint-plugin-react-hooks` 7.1.1's `memo-dependencies` and two more rules over `src/artifacts` and `src/renderer`) found no other shipped case: ElementBar ships uncompiled only because the compiler fails on the file, and WidgetDialog and ModelChip are latent and unreachable, each a residue row with the hook-lint gap in `src/artifacts` (53 findings). Rows: L213 ("portable compiled coverage", 2026-10-18) closes with W2-D's project, and the 2026-10-19 deck inspector row narrows to its fractional widths, its stale-until-reselected half being this bug. Gates: class 1a green at 74c4c5376 (39592604575); presentations and design in the integration's gates (Decision Log 55): the whole Mac set 39599854252 and the measured `e2e:presentations-scale` 39599930074 on the gated tree 3abdf3c57, `e2e:settings` re-gated after its fix as 39659173748 on 14f14519165, class 1a 39630489726 on the landing chain's tree. Review: reviewer-medium over 923cafc22..74c4c5376, no material findings. Three residue rows. Its brief is carried in the W2 ledger, as P4-R5's was.

78. **2026-10-24 (the architect, master 8911ed6c9), UX1 broke the desk's browser suite and its gate never ran it; fixed and landed.** UX2-L's whole-matrix run found `e2e:desk-web` red, and master control 40091211614 (d21563173) showed the same 20 failures (176 passed). The last green desk run was 39893304199 on b4f141b2c. The desk web reuses MAWS's transcript and report view, and UX1-C changed it on purpose: closed-tag ink unboxed instead of message cards (fafc693c7, l2-transcript-03), tool summaries visible with the outer tool-group fold removed (5ab559bcd, l2-transcript-01), authored line breaks preserved (7f686dc35, l2-transcript-03), which makes long answers tall enough to leave the virtualized window. UX1's integration gate ran default, settings, c7, transcript, composer and to-human, not desk-web, and not c12, whose Unread selector also went red with UX1-B's unread toggle (master control 40092803901; UX2-L carries the one-line fix). The fix branch `desk-web-ux1-fix` (astra; ffd673b80, effc1b432, b97a2b1ff) updated the desk's tests to UX1-C's intended rendering, and found one real mismatch: the desk synthesised closing tags and fences for a still-streaming row, while MAWS's own live view shows an unfinished tag literally (`owner/to-human/Presenter.tsx:7-10`, pinned by `Presenter.test.tsx` with `live=true`) yet folds the turn on an open mark (`owner/report/project.ts:36-40`). The repair was removed and the shared report projection now also reads open marks inline on external live rows, never on rows a native live buffer owns. So the phone shows a raw opening `<message>` until its close arrives, exactly as MAWS's native view does; the owner was told, and with to-human 0.7.0 the mod draws messages in MAWS. Gates: 40096475967 on b97a2b1ff green (static, probes, four unit shards, desk-web 196 passed with one existing WebKit skip); Mac transcript 35/35 in 40094832027 on the identical product tree. reviewer-medium converged over three rounds. Landed by merge commit 8911ed6c9 (master had moved docs-only). Rule from this, for every integration brief: the Mac groups a wave runs are chosen by what the changed code feeds, not by the batches' own groups: shared transcript code means transcript and desk-web; shared shell, layout or sidebar code means the whole Mac matrix. UX2's integration runs the whole matrix.

79. **2026-10-24 (the architect, master c50cf233e), UX2 lands: the layout redesign, two batches integrated and gated on the whole Mac matrix.** UX2-L (the shell, p4-ux2l cbfc32057) built D1, D2, D3, D4 and D9 and the owner's stacking ruling: main owns the space with no empty panel reservation; surfaces open only when chosen, two stacked at most, plain clicks stack, close or replace the lower slot; one chrome row; four folded floating cards with Artifacts as the project-scoped Library; wide automatic widths only for Review, Files and Browser (60%), every other surface a stable 420 px automatic width clamped by the main-area floor, explicit widths kept; the sidebar yields below 1100 when a surface is open and a surface becomes a dismissible sheet below 700; one More menu in the session header; a document's tools once, in its own toolbar, Arrange always a menu, the filmstrip below the stage when the editor is narrower than 1100 and at the left otherwise (mockup app.js:366 and 380); the usage pill in the sidebar footer; a searchable shortcut sheet and F6 region traversal; sessions on shift-command brackets, tabs on option-command brackets, control-Tab cycling; additive `panelTools.surfaces` persistence that keeps existing layouts. UX2-K (p4-ux2k 87f222280) built the sidebar's top (one Search field over UX1's live filter, Enter to the palette at All, no wordmark), the Projects page, the Customize tab (Plugins, Skills, MCP servers by project or user config, from a read-only disk inventory that starts no session and passes no secret) and the composer's Connectors and Plugins submenus. An astra integrator merged both on p4-ux2 (c50cf233e) by merge commits; the collisions were in tests only (K's acceptance fixture forced a collapsed panel that L's model no longer has; L's keyboard test expected Search then Needs you with K's four destinations now between; a shared row helper matched a transcript question row). Gates on the final head: class 1a and the Mac default group 40107395448 green, settings 40104953998 green; the whole Mac matrix 40104052208 ran all 35 groups, desk-web included, 32 green, and its three reds passed on follow-ups (default 40107395448, browser-cua 40106773118 with a race kept as debt, design-systems 40111407215, which also ran class 1a green on the final head); the merged branch fast-forwarded master. reviewer-medium on the integration's own changes found nothing material over two rounds. Found on the way: UX1's integration had also left the tools group red on master (Needs you's reason moved into the row's accessible name, so a text filter matched nothing; carried on L); a shared Radix submenu fault, a submenu flipped left at the window edge closing on the pointer's exit, fixed once for nine SubTriggers (src/artifacts/ui/menu-pointer.ts) with the Export and Library pointer paths unchanged as proof; a test-harness cleanup race on master (a63d651ca); the layout-transfer case red on master since UX1 now passes under L's accounting, but Expand gains no width at 1280 or 1512 (620 to 620, 852 to 852 with the 420 conversation reservation), which is the owner's question at acceptance; a completion toast over the visible Browser hides the whole native view, on master too (control 40101257776), so the test closes it and the placement is debt. Lens 7: l7-shell-01 to 05 closed, 12 kept; 07, 09, 10, 11 and 13 partly; 08 not built. Open: the owner's hands-on acceptance and the Expand question. The ledger `docs/doperpowers/ledgers/2026-10-24-P4-UX2.md` carries the batches' records.

80. **2026-10-24 (the architect, master 0acb77912), a pinned comment card now re-pins after its artifact frame is replaced (PR #139).** e2e:side failed intermittently after UX2 landed, on the guest (40112861862) and on the exclusive host (40118458114, case 11 after a Reload; 40120793945, case 12 after a v2 to v1 switch), while untouched master passed on the host (40118919090, 40119072844). Two remount triggers failing the same way pointed at the product: CardHost's locate retried a null or timed-out answer only for a card never located, so a card pinned before kept its old frame's coordinates when the new frame's first bounded ask (HtmlFrame's 1 s bridge budget) was lost, and nothing re-asked after the frame loaded. The fix republishes a fresh locate from HtmlFrame on every load and bridge-ready, so the re-ask is event-driven, and gives an already pinned card one bounded null retry; stale answers stay ignored. Three regressions failed before the change; 144 focused tests pass. The side spec now keeps trace, screenshot and geometry for both cases (the red reports had carried none). Gates on 90e674f13: class 1a 40121990836, host side 40121984354, 40121987013, 40121989082, all green, the captured geometry exactly 4 px under the heading in both cases. reviewer-medium converged after one fix (evidence capture could skip cleanup). A user could have seen a comment card left in the wrong place after reloading an artifact or switching its version.

81. **2026-10-24 (the architect, master 64cbab57b), the owner's rulings on Expand and the hands-on acceptance of UX1 and UX2; the fix wave.** The owner ruled on Expand: it folds the conversation, as Codex does, into a compressed composer-only view that reopens, so the document takes the main area; an astra worker builds it on p4-ux2-expand. The owner approved a hands-on acceptance on their screen; an opus agent drove a separate build (worktree /tmp/maws-accept, profile /tmp/maws-accept-profile, bound by app path) through cua, 19:13 to 19:50Z. Most of UX1 and UX2 passed as built: Needs you, rows, filter, chords, the transcript, stacking, widths, overflow and pinning, cards, document tools, the sidebar top, Projects and Customize. Thirteen defects, ranked by what the owner would notice: the hover card's Reply on a waiting question queues a follow-up instead of answering; overflowing tabs vanish; the composer footer clips in a narrow column; the sheet below 700 does not dismiss; the conversation-search row sits after hundreds of uncapped results; the Questions surface stays open empty; the Overview button disappears at 900 with a surface open; MAWS's own ask tool arrives in Manual mode as a raw-JSON approval; Priority sort reorders rows under the pointer; stale panel vocabulary in the shortcut sheet; the transcript does not follow a send while scrolled up; F6 skips the cards and lands on a divider; and small ones. A further list passes but spends space against the owner's taste (a double fold, repeated names, chrome rows above documents and decks, discovered folders crowding Projects). One astra lead owns all of it on p4-ux-accept with the whole Mac matrix: defects and economies inside the rulings are fixed; a change to the approved design itself comes back as an owner question with a proposal. Not checkable on an unsigned build: OS notification banners, Reply and Allow once. The report is `docs/doperpowers/evidence/p4/ux-wave/acceptance-2026-10-24.md`, the screenshots in MAWS-notes/ux-comparison-2026-10-23/acceptance-2026-10-24/.

82. **2026-10-24 (the architect, master 81011dd09), the restore banner's read race fixed in both history controllers (PR #141).** CI-opt's guest trial of presentations (run 40123121420) showed the banner after a restore reading 'Restored from an earlier version' where the version list already carried 'Restored from Draft'. In the deck controller a newer onVersions read advanced the read counter, so the restore's own refresh was not published and `restored()` looked the label up in a stale snapshot; the document controller had the same snapshot lookup, open to an older overlapping response. Both refresh methods now return their own read and the banner takes its label from it; publication guards unchanged. Regressions order the two reads with deferred promises and failed before the fix (deck 10 of 11, document 6 of 7). Gates on head 22c220696: 40125301159 (class 1a, document in the guest) and 40128086600 (presentations on the host) green; reviewer-low found nothing material. The first run's presentations red was presentations-export.spec.ts:620 (two distinct PNGs where three were expected), untouched code, green on the unchanged rerun; logged as debt.

83. **2026-10-24 (the architect, master 673c67f41), Expand lands as the owner ruled: the conversation folds into the compact composer.** Replacing the full floated card of Decision Log 40 and the UX2 behaviour, Expand now presents MAWS's existing compact composer (WR #59) at the lower right and releases the conversation reservation; the session title on it reopens the full conversation, the draft survives, Restore split returns the ratio and the panel. Measured in the harness: the document goes from 309 px in the split to 1040 at 1280x820 and from 425 to 1272 at 1512x982, 420 px over UX2's alone-main width, with a zero conversation column. Codex's readable build (content-chat-surface) separates a composer-only presentation from the transcript the same way; Claude Desktop's pane controls have no equivalent fold. Choices made in place: no new persisted state (the compact presentation is transient), no new shortcut, a fresh actionable request reopens the full conversation and takes focus only when the compact composer had it. p4-ux2-expand (759a8d68a, 0b88e06ba) by an astra worker: class 1a 40124998019 green; whole Mac matrix 40124518939 33 of 35 groups, presentations (export PNG distinctness) and tools (empty preview fontFamily) red, both green on the head's replay 40133987363 and on the master control 40133985981, so intermittent; reviewer-medium converged with nothing material. The zero-gain Expand debt row closes; the 2026-10-20 relaunch-evidence row stays.

## Approval ask (what the owner must see before the cut is approved)

Answered by the owner at 21:30Z on 2026-10-19 (Decision Log 17): item 1 approved; item 2, the verdict is a close criterion (Desktop faster by more than the threshold opens A3, and P4 closes only at parity); item 4, in B2 if its plan bounds them, else the decks round; item 9, the owner's user scope; items 3, 5 to 8, 10 and 11 stood as presented with no objection.

1. **The cut itself:** eleven children (A1, A2, A3 conditional, B1 to B5, W, L, F), the contracts X33 to X39, the ordering of §7 and the close of §2.
2. **The parity verdict as a close criterion, or not.** The charter's P4 paragraph names "not slower than Claude Desktop on the same edit tasks" as a target of the measure; this spec runs the bench as one bounded question (five repetitions, the 1.2 × threshold) whose verdict decides whether the edit-protocol child A3 exists. The owner chooses: the verdict stays a target of the measure (parity required for the close), or it is recorded and only A3 depends on it (Decision Log 10, 12, 16.10).
3. **The bench's measurement-only cells:** where Desktop's deck kinds run only in Cowork (effort `medium`, its own system prompt), MAWS's arm for those cells is pinned to Desktop's setting for the measurement; no effort decision follows (Decision Log 8, 16.2).
4. **The canvas accessibility roles** (obs 5's "접근성 트리? DOM? 이 너무 구리다"): in B2 if its plan bounds them, else the decks surface's round; the owner should know this part may slip to the round (16.16).
5. **T5, the one-gesture double-click into grouped text and table cells:** the owner's chosen departure from Canva's level-entry ("더블클릭하면 그룹이나 표 안에 있어도 가장 안쪽 텍스트로 바로 들어가요"); confirmed as chosen, since it changes what a double-click on any group means (Decision Log 6, 16.4).
6. **Escape keeps a non-empty comment draft** (Decision Log 5): a departure from Google Docs, which cancels; Word desktop's rule.
7. **Both floating cards at the bottom right** (Decision Log 15.7): the panel card moves from its P3 bottom-left corner to align with the split card.
8. **The accounts the live checks and the bench reach:** the owner's Google, Canva and Figma accounts inside the folder "MAWS UX check"; a Notion test page and a Drive test folder for the delivery surface; the owner's Anthropic account for the bench's Desktop runs in one project the architect names; each deleted after, no sharing, no invites, breaking confined to MAWS (Decision Log 6, 15.15, 16.9).
9. **The assessors' build runs on the owner's user scope** (the owner's plugins, output style and hooks reach it like any MAWS session) with MAWS's own user data isolated (16.9).
10. **The 80 responses:** L's first round waits for them; if they have not arrived when A to C land, the architect asks before L starts (Decision Log 3, 16.13).
11. **The surfaces' order and pace:** documents, decks, designs and design systems, dashboards, the library, the shell, the transcript; two at a time (Decision Log 15.6).
